Extracted from the code as it stood on 2026-10-01/02 by a stage-1 agent. Nothing here has been checked against its source. Paths are relative to the repository root; line numbers are left out on purpose.

Notation. "default" is the Rust `TectonicParams::default()` value; "quick_16k" is `crates/deeptime-core/presets/earthlike_quick_16k.json`, which is identical to `pipeline_quick_16k/32k` and `earthlike_quick_32k` for the tectonics section. `earthlike_full_16k/32k` and `pipeline_full_16k/32k` differ only in `subduction_uplift_mm_yr` 0.34 and `collision_uplift_max_m` 1100. All four `plates/plates_*` presets are identical to each other and carry exactly the Rust default values (they omit the keys added later and inherit the default). The pipeline presets differ from the default in 16 keys, listed in each row's value cell. Cortial = Cortial, Peytavie, Galin & Guerin 2019, "Procedural Tectonic Planets", Computer Graphics Forum 38(2). "P1-03 log" = STATUS.md build-log entry "2026-09-28 P1-03 plates and crust". "P1-17 log" = "2026-10-01 P1-17 hypsometry recalibration 2026-10-01". "P1-27 log" = "2026-09-30 P1-27 plate margins and coherent continents". "calib 09-29 log" = "2026-09-29 P1-17 partial: calibration with the real climate".

Who decided a `game-choice`: a row whose "citation written in" names the decisions file or a decisions-log date was decided by Ben on that date; every other `game-choice` row is an implementation decision by the agent that wrote it, recorded in the build log entry the row names (P1-03 = 2026-09-28, P1-27 = 2026-09-30, P1-17 = 2026-10-01) or only in the code ("agent, no log entry" when the row names none). Rows in `designed, not yet coded` describe settings in design documents; their "value" is the proposed default.

### plate motion

| PLT-022 | plate motion | Speed guard: after the relaxation towards the force balance a plate's rotation rate is scaled down so no point moves faster; every plate-step it binds is counted (`plates/guard_bound_plate_steps`, the dynamics record's guard flag) and plate-check fails if it ever binds. Until the switch (2026-10-04) a hard cap at Cortial's v_0 = 100 mm/yr that the speed rules leaned on. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.max_plate_speed_mm_yr; plates/mod.rs (motion); plates/kinematics.rs: cap_speed | 250.0 (default and every preset; was 100) | mm/yr | none (a guard that should never bind; design docs/design/plate-force-balance.md sections 6 and 11) | code comment; decisions log 2026-10-02 (the coordinator kept it as a reported guard) | `game-choice`. At a speed scale of 75 (PLT-181) it bound in 3 of 8 runs while the force balance read fronts on the frozen lattice (arm sp-v75, 2026-10-26), and in none of 8 once PLT-582 reached the balance (arm fx582-v75, 2026-10-26; plates.md "The force balance's front on the current geometry (2026-10-26)"). |
| PLT-023 | plate motion | Retired with the switch to the force balance (force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04): each plate's rotation relaxes towards the force balance's answer. Successor: PLT-191. Was: Each step a plate's speed relaxes towards a target set by its subduction front length, so slab-attached plates speed up and the others drift. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/mod.rs: PlatesStage::advance (motion block) | relax = 1 - exp(-dt / slab_pull_relax_myr); slab = slab_pull_speed x min(front_km / slab_pull_front_km, 1); target = max(slab, base) x (1 - (1 - continental_speed_factor) x continental_frac); v_new = v + (target - v) x relax | mm/yr | none cited for the law (Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 is cited only for the qualitative claim, see PLT-030) | code comment; P1-03 log design choice 6 | `merged` (retired, force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04; successor PLT-191) |
| PLT-024 | plate motion | Retired with the switch to the force balance (force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04): a plate nothing pulls is moved by ridge push, suction, its neighbours and the weak mantle flow. Successor: PLT-190. Was: Plates with no subduction front relax to the middle of the initial speed range, a speed with no physical driver (the force-balance design lists it as a rule to remove). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/mod.rs: base = 0.5 x (plate_speed_mm_yr[0] + [1]) | 45.0 mm/yr from [20, 70] | mm/yr | none cited | code only; design doc docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 2 ("a plate with no slab moves at 45 mm/yr for no reason") | `merged` (retired, force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04; successor PLT-190) |
| PLT-025 | plate motion | Retired with the switch to the force balance (force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04): continents are slower through their keels' drag (keel_drag). Successor: PLT-188. Was: Continental plates move slower: the target speed is multiplied by this factor for an all-continental plate, interpolated by continental share. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.continental_speed_factor; plates/init.rs and mod.rs | 0.6 (default and every preset): multiplier = 1 - 0.4 x continental_frac | dimensionless | none cited (Earth contrast quoted only in the force-balance design: continental plates 26-28 mm/yr against about 85 for the fastest group, Zahirovic et al. 2015) | code comment; design doc docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 2 | `merged` (retired, force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04; successor PLT-188) |
| PLT-026 | plate motion | The earlier motion that ages the starting ocean floor (PLT-273): an Euler pole drawn at random but at least 30 degrees from the plate's centroid so it translates rather than spins; the rate gives the drawn speed at the centroid. Since the switch (2026-10-04) it moves nothing: plates start at rest. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/kinematics.rs: random_motion | pole accepted when sin(angle to centroid) > 0.5 (16 tries); rate = omega_of_speed(v) / max(sin, 0.3) | rad/yr | none cited | code comment | `game-choice` |
| PLT-027 | plate motion | Retired with the switch to the force balance (force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04): the random walk is replaced by the mantle flow. Successor: PLT-190. Was: Euler poles take a small random walk each step, standing in for mantle reorganisation. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.motion_jitter_per_sqrt_myr; plates/kinematics.rs: jitter | 0.02 per sqrt(Myr) relative change; omega += gaussian vector x 0.02 x sqrt(dt) x length of omega | dimensionless | none cited | code comment | `merged` (retired, force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04; successor PLT-190) |
| PLT-028 | plate motion | Plates move rigidly: each step every group turns by the finite rotation of angle (rotation rate x dt) about its Euler pole, and surface velocity is omega cross position. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/kinematics.rs: rotation, advance; normalise.rs: Norm::speed_mm_yr | v = length(omega x p) x R_model x 1e6 mm/yr; rotation angle = length(omega) x dt | mm/yr | "Standard kinematics (the GPlates concepts), no code ported"; Cortial section 3 (s(p) = omega w x p) | code comment; plan section 8.1 | `cited-unverified`, `pending-rewrite` |

### subduction

| PLT-029 | subduction | Retired with the switch to the force balance (force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04): slab pull is a force at the trench. Successor: PLT-182. Was: Slab pull on direction: a subducting plate's rotation axis turns towards its subduction fronts by this much per 1,000 km of front each Myr. Since plate tasks 3 and 4, 2026-10-02 only fronts where the subducting crust is oceanic count (PLT-251). | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.slab_pull_rad_per_1000km_myr; plates/mod.rs; plates/kinematics.rs: pull_axis | 0.002 (default and every preset); axis += sum(c cross q normalised x front_km/1000) x 0.002 x dt | rad per 1000 km per Myr | Cortial section 4.1 (epsilon, scaled here by front length) | code comment | `merged` (retired, force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04; successor PLT-182) |
| PLT-030 | subduction | Retired with the switch to the force balance (force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04): slab pull is a force at the trench, and slab resistance sets the slab-attached plates' speed. Successor: PLT-182. Was: Slab pull on speed: a plate with at least the full front length relaxes to this speed at its centroid (plates attached to slabs move several times faster). Since plate tasks 3 and 4, 2026-10-02 only fronts where the subducting crust is oceanic count (PLT-251). | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.slab_pull_speed_mm_yr | 80.0 (default and every preset) | mm/yr | Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 (plates attached to slabs move several times faster) | code comment | `merged` (retired, force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04; successor PLT-182) |
| PLT-031 | subduction | Retired with the switch to the force balance (force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04): a slab pulls by its tracked length. Successor: PLT-184. Was: Front length that gives the full slab-pull speed; shorter fronts give proportionally less. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.slab_pull_front_km | 3000.0 (default and every preset) | km | none cited | code comment | `merged` (retired, force-balance task 7, 2026-10-04; successor PLT-184) |

| PLT-045 | subduction | New crust an island arc (an arc on oceanic crust above an oceanic slab) adds per km of arc per Myr at Earth's mean orthogonal convergence of intra-oceanic arcs (86.6 mm/yr, PLT-666), scaled by v_n / 86.6 with v_n the convergence normal to the front and no cap (PLT-665; until 2026-10-25 at v0 = 100 mm/yr, scaled by g = min(v / v0, 1) of the whole relative speed, PLT-037, which counted Earth's convergence twice): magma intruded and erupted, spread over the arc band (Gaussian of half-width `arc_width_km` 60 km centred `arc_distance_km` 200 km from the front; its integral across the arc is √π x 60 = 106 km), into the pools by PLT-046, less the volcano stage's felsic cones on the arc (PLT-286). The arc stands up by the crust added, on the ocean's height line (PLT-264), towards and never past the floor its crust holds up. Only an oceanic slab makes an arc (continental crust forced under at a collision makes none). Until plate task 8 (2026-10-17): `arc_magma_m_per_myr` 20 m/Myr at the axis (about 2.1 km3/km/Myr, 25 to 90 times below Earth's) with a height-only uplift `arc_uplift_mm_yr` (HYP-017). | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.island_arc_km3_per_km_myr; plates/subduction.rs: fronts_and_uplift (island_axis_m, Plan.magma_m) | 100.0 (default and every preset); 940 m/Myr at the axis at v_n = 86.6 mm/yr | km3 per km of arc per Myr | Stern & Scholl 2010 (full text, author copy, read 2026-10-17), p. 11: intra-oceanic arc growth "approximately 100 km3/km-million years (500 km3/million years for first 10 million years plus 30 km3/million years for 60 million years thereafter). At this rate, crustal growth at the present 14,500 km of intra-oceanic arcs" gives 1.5 AU (Table 1, p. 12); p. 9: Izu-Bonin-Mariana and the Aleutians "90-180 km3/km-million years". Lower: Parcutela et al. 2023 (full text): the Western Pacific island arcs "28-60 km3/km/m.y." from gravity, other oceanic arcs less; Clift et al. 2009 (full text, p. 84): "oceanic arcs average rates of 125 km3/Ma/km compared to 81 km3/Ma/km for continental arcs". Jicha & Jagoutz 2015 not obtained. The long-term average was taken, so a young arc builds slower and an old one faster than Earth's front-loaded arcs; `arc_front_loaded` (PLT-668, built 2026-10-25, off) gives the island arcs S&S's front-loaded law instead. | code comment; build log 2026-10-17 "Plate task 8"; decisions log 2026-10-17 ("Plate task 7: the crust budget on sourced rates") | `verified` (Stern & Scholl 2010 p. 11, the value read; earlier flag: measured 2026-10-16, plate task 7, island arcs added 0.14 km3 a year against Earth's 1.5; measured 2026-10-17 (plate task 8, `earthlike_quick_16k`, 500 Myr, six runs; 1000 Myr, eight runs): island arcs add 3.96 and 4.41 km3 a year against Earth's 1.5, and 31.7 and 31.4 km3 per km of oceanic front per Myr (the cones counted in the rate not included): the model's fronts are long (about 125,000 km against Earth's 14,500), not its rate (plates.md, "The crust budget on sourced rates")) |

| PLT-047 | subduction | Island-arc crust becomes continental crust once it is thick enough, whatever its height: a visible oceanic sample turns continental when the arc crust it has received (`arc_oceanic_m`, its cones included) reaches `arc_mature_crust_km` less the ocean's own 7 km, i.e. 8 km of arc crust on 7 km of ocean crust. Counted on the arc crust, not the column's thickness, because a resampling's stacked crust also sits under the oceanic label. Since 2026-10-18 ocean floor at a contact with a whole continental column's crystalline crust (33 km), however it got there, turns continental by PLT-289. Until plate task 8 (2026-10-17) the setting was `arc_maturity_myr` 20 (time at the old 20 m/Myr arc rate, which was not a physical thickness), before 2026-10-02 `arc_continental_m` (metres; 80 in the pipeline presets, 0 in the plate-only ones, tuned to keep the continental crust near 0.40, P1-27), when the sample also had to stand above sea level and be an active arc that step. Since 2026-10-20 the ocean crust under the arc stays under it as its basement (PLT-331): the column turns continental, and only the arc's own crust counts as continental crust. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.arc_mature_crust_km; plates/mod.rs: finish_samples; plates/state.rs: Sample::make_continental | 15.0 (default and every preset): 8 km of arc crust; at the island-arc rate (PLT-045) 8.5 Myr on the arc axis at full convergence, longer at slower trenches or off the axis | km | Cloos 1993 (GSA Bulletin 105:715-737), abstract: "only bodies of continental and oceanic island arc crust that are > ~15 km thick make the lithosphere buoyant enough to jam a subduction zone. Oceanic island arc complexes built upon ocean crust typically must be active for more than ~20 m.y. to attain crustal thicknesses so that their attempted subduction causes collisional orogenesis." (Tetreault & Buiter 2014, read in full, repeat the 15 km and note that the paucity of whole island-arc sections in the record disagrees with it.) Now that the arc rate is a physical thickness (PLT-045), the rule takes Cloos's thickness; his 20 Myr then follows from the rate: an axis sample at full convergence reaches it in 8.5 Myr, one at half the speed in 17. Height is not the criterion: "intra-oceanic arcs are generally mostly below sea level" (Stern 2010, p. 9, read in full) | code comment; build log 2026-10-02 "Arc emergence by crust" and 2026-10-17 "Plate task 8"; decisions log 2026-10-02 ("Investigate, then fix arc emergence") | `abstract-only` (Cloos 1993 abstract read via OpenAlex, 2026-10-02; the 15 km is his "> ~15 km") |
| PLT-048 | subduction | The arc's own cordillera (continental crust over an arc above an oceanic slab) thickens by crust shortened into it. Pure shear: a range sample of the overriding plate (continental, nearer the front than `arc_distance_km` + 2 x `cordillera_width_km` = 440 km) takes in its thickness x the strain, which is the shortening per step over the band's integral (√π x `cordillera_width_km`) times the band's Gaussian and the room left below `cordillera_height_m` (PLT-050). The volume comes from the same plate's continental crust behind the range (440 km to r_s = 800 km), which loses the same share of every pool, at most the shortening over its own width (with `tectonics.thermal_backstop`, 2026-10-23, off by default as a stand-in, each source's share weighted by its lithosphere's weakness, PLT-470), and the range receives the mix of pools it gave: volume kept. Both stand up or sink by their Airy change, towards and never past the column's Airy height (`collapse::iso_height`). With `tectonics.margin_slab_ends` (PLT-562, on since 2026-10-24) the range and its sources are only the samples over the front's slab: past a front's ends no range and no crust shortened into it. Until plate task 8 (2026-10-17): `cordillera_uplift_mm_yr` (0.3 in the pipeline presets, calibrated so the ground under the cones stood 497 to 749 m) with an Airy root made from nothing (PLT-051), which added about 8.5 km3 a year of continental crust against Earth's 1.0. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.cordillera_shortening_mm_yr; plates/subduction.rs: fronts_and_uplift (Plan.demand_m, Budget.supply_m, phi, met) | default and plate-only presets 0.0; pipeline, earthlike and planet_world_4k 1.7 (6.0 and scaled by g = min(v / v0, 1) until 2026-10-20) | mm/yr | Since 2026-10-20 (PLT-320): Heuret & Lallemand 2005 (PEPI 149:31-51, full text, read 2026-10-20), Table 1: of 88 two-degree segments of continental upper plates, 21 are class C3, "lithospheric scale back-arc thrusting", and their measured back-arc deformation rate averages 7.1 mm/yr (14 with a value); the rest shorten little or extend, so the length-weighted mean is 1.7 mm/yr (21 x 7.1 / 88, the agent's arithmetic on the table). Until then: Eichelberger et al. 2015 (EPSL 426:191-203, full text, read 2026-10-17): Bolivia has "the largest shortening magnitudes (~300 km, 40%) and crustal thicknesses (up to 75 km) of the entire chain", restored "from the restored state (50 Ma) to the modern state (0 Ma)": 300 km in 50 Myr is 6 mm/yr; "Assuming pure shear deformation, the decrease in area leads to a proportional increase in predicted thickness" (constant volume). Shortening alone falls short of the Andes' crust: "known shortening at the surface accounts for only 70-80% of the observed crustal thickening" (Allmendinger et al. 1997, abstract; Kley & Monaldi 1998, abstract, the same question); Haschke & Günther 2003 (abstract) balance an arc's thickening about 2:1 tectonic to magmatic. The arc magma (PLT-285) is the rest. Applying the chain's largest rate at every continental arc above an oceanic slab, and taking the volume from the plate's continental crust behind the range, are the model's. | code comment; build log 2026-10-17 "Plate task 8" | `verified` (since 2026-10-20 the value is the mean of Heuret & Lallemand 2005's Table 1 over continental arcs, the table read, PLT-320; before it Eichelberger et al. 2015, the shortening and its age read; its use at every arc and the source zone are `game-choice`, agent, plate task 8; measured 2026-10-17 (plate task 8): 3.22 km3 a year shortened into the ranges at 500 Myr and 3.48 at 1000 (moved, not made); the median continental arc cell stands 1,095 m against master's 2,055 (plate-check, 1000 Myr; target 1,200 to 4,500)) |

The sources read in the plate-geometry task (2026-10-21) contradict it: Crameri et al. 2020 (full text and Supplementary Data 1): the subducting plates' mean ages at the 13 initiations since 104 Ma are 32 to 117 Myr, "At least nine of all thirteen events ... occur at or near a pre-existing convergent plate boundary", "subduction breeds subduction"; Gurnis et al. 2004 (full text, Table 1): eleven Cenozoic initiations, each at a fracture zone, a transform, a spreading centre, an extinct subduction zone or by polarity reversal; Hall et al. 2003 (abstract): a fracture zone becomes self-sustaining after about 100 km of convergence | code comment (plates/rifting.rs doc) | `cited-unverified`, `pending-rewrite` (the model uses the rule to recycle old floor far from trenches; moving initiation near trenches (PLT-064's note) was measured and not kept, 2026-10-21) |

Changed P1-19 #27 (2026-10-18): was counted per step; now per Myr (or a time), so a coarser plate step keeps the rate per year; at the 2-Myr step the value is the old one exactly.; its share between plates by their old floor alone replaced by default since 2026-10-23 by PLT-505's compression weight (this rate stays the hazard's size; the old share runs with `initiation_stress_mpa` 0). Note (the front lane, 2026-10-25; the rate left as it is, the coordinator's call): Crameri et al. 2020 (full text, lines 340 to 354 of the library's text, right column) place where initiations happen, not how often: "At least nine of all thirteen events identified in the current version of the database occur at or near a pre-existing convergent plate boundary (see Fig. 5a, d). In fact, two of the most common characteristics of all SZI events in the SZI database are initiation along a pre-existing boundary and initiation near a pre-existing subduction zone (see Fig. 5d). This often close association between SZI and pre-existing boundaries (especially subduction zones) is further demonstrated by quantitative investigations of plate reconstructions presented in the 'Methods' section. All SZI events start less than 1500 km away from other active subduction zones and, in most cases, less than 500 km. Transform faults are also often present in the proximity of the SZI event location. Therefore, pre-existing subduction zones and transform faults seem to be the major players for a SZI event or they create favourable conditions for a new subduction zone to develop." This hazard is by old oceanic area, weighted by compression (PLT-505), with no term for the distance to a subduction zone or a transform |
| PLT-064 | subduction | Old crust can start subducting only this far from every trench (a new zone does not open beside a working one). | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.subduction_initiation_clearance_km | 1000.0 (default and every preset), Earth-normalised; one lattice spacing in its place with `tectonics.margin_propagation` on (PLT-656, off by default since 2026-10-26) | km | none cited. Contradicted by Crameri et al. 2020 (full text, read 2026-10-21): "All SZI events start less than 1500 km away from other active subduction zones and, in most cases, less than 500 km" | code comment; P1-27 log; build log 2026-10-21 "The plate geometry" | `game-choice`, `pending-rewrite` (2026-10-21, the plate-geometry task: initiation within 1,500 km of a trench instead (arm i1, `earthlike_quick_16k`, 1000 Myr, seeds 1 to 3, both starts, against master c790d1f3) raised the initiations from 7.0 to 12.7 per 100 Myr (Crameri: 13 since 104 Ma) but split off slivers: plates absorbed 7.7 to 12.4 per 100 Myr, median plate life 129 to 81 Myr, 16.3 % dead within 20 Myr (at most 15), floor older than 250 Myr 3.2 to 4.4 % (at most 3), supercontinent tenure 126 to 39 Myr, plate checks 31 to 26 of 45, land 0.264 to 0.235. Not kept: it needs laws for a trench to propagate and a slab to roll back. Replaced 2026-10-25 with the floor-age lane's law 2 (PLT-656), which Crameri et al. 2020 source together with it, by the coordinator's order: with `tectonics.margin_propagation` on no initiation within one lattice spacing of a trench (`rifting::maybe_initiate_subduction`); the 1,000 km holds with law 2 off. Law 2 was parked 2026-10-26 (off by default: it never advances in the sim), so the 1,000 km is the default again, by the coordinator's call, and the rewrite stays pending. The records count `initiations_near_fronts`, the initiations whose seed lies within the old 1,000 km) |

In effect it does not (V1 gap 2's measurement, 2026-10-23, base 7f9d8f115049, 24 runs): the Voronoi split runs along the continent's edge where the old floor lies against it, so the old floor goes down under the continent and 0.173 of the coast (of 0.312 at a subduction front) is at a front an initiation made. ### plate-check targets

Each ranged measure of `deeptime plate-check` is a claim about what a good plate model does. The code takes the mean over the runs given and passes it when it lies inside the range, both ends included. Ranges are from `docs/design/plate-collisions-and-welding.md` section 6, which `check.rs` says is their source. Baseline values (mean of seeds 1-4, master fa09e8b, 1000 Myr) are in the earth file.

| PLT-143 | plate-check targets | Target for the largest plate's median share of the planet. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/check.rs: MEASURES largest_share_p50 | lo 0.15, hi 0.35 | share | Earth 0.20 (Pacific plate 103.3 million km2): P. Bird 2003, Geochem. Geophys. Geosyst. 4(3):1027, areas from en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tectonic_plates; design doc also says "No source gives the largest plate's share through a supercontinent's life. From memory: the plate carrying Pangaea was well under half the planet" | design doc docs/design/plate-collisions-and-welding.md sections 2.2, 3 and 6 | `verified` for today's figure (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): Bird 2003 Table 1 gives the Pacific 2.57685 sr, 20.5 % of the sphere (full text, author's site, library Bird2003.txt). The range's width and the remark on Pangaea's plate stay from memory. `pending-rewrite` removed: the check stands as the rewrite's acceptance test (claims.md fix 41) |

Source search of 2026-10-22 (coordinator's research agent) found no source for 2 to 6 or for 3 to 4. What the measure counts: a collision whose terrane is at least `collision_min_frac` (0.1 of a starting plate's area) and that ends in a weld or a transfer, dated at its end (plates/collision.rs `collide`); smaller transfers are `terrane_accretion` events, not counted. Earth figures found: Macdonald et al. 2019, Science 364:181-184 (read in full): six peaks of total suture length in 540 Myr for the major orogenies (about 1.1 per 100 Myr), and 15 to 46 ophiolite-bearing arc-continent sutures per 100 Myr (Paleozoic 44 in 290 Myr, Mesozoic 32 in 185, Cenozoic 30 in 65), which match the model's terrane accretions rather than its collisions; Condie, Pisarevsky, Korenaga and Gardoll 2015, Precambrian Research 259:278-289: "about one to 20 per 100 Myr, with an average of six" for continental plate collisions over 2.5 Gyr, and very low rates in 150-0 Ma (search-engine extract only; the paper was not read and is not in deeptime-sources, so unverified) | design doc plate-collisions-and-welding.md section 2.2; STATUS.md P1-10 row ("about 10 collisions per 100 Myr vs 3-4; log 2026-10-01") | `uncited`; `pending-rewrite` removed in WP-13a (claims.md fix 41: the check stands as the rewrite's acceptance test) |

### designed, not yet coded

Nothing in this group exists in the Rust code yet (checked: no `forces.rs`, no `slab_depth_km`, `weld_speed_mm_yr`, `collision_belt_km` or `wedge_taper_deg` in `params.rs`). The collisions design (`docs/design/plate-collisions-and-welding.md`) was decided 2026-10-02 and is extended by the force-balance design (`docs/design/plate-force-balance.md`), which supersedes it where they differ. Both say "design, nothing built". The force-balance design's section 12 says its sources were "checked online on 2026-10-02 by a sub-agent; most papers are paywalled, so figures are from abstracts or secondary pages unless a link is given. Items it could not confirm are marked in the text."

| PLT-181 | force balance | The one overall speed scale of the force balance: the RMS plate speed the balance is normalised to. | docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 6 (settings) | plate_speed_scale 75 (45 until 2026-10-26) | mm/yr | Earth: area-weighted RMS about 47 mm/yr today; 50-100 mm/yr over the last 200 Myr (WP-13a) | design doc plate-force-balance.md sections 6 and 7 | `calibrated` (2026-10-26: 75 against CHK-020, read with PLT-582 in the force balance, arm fx582-v75 at the end of this cell). Earlier `verified` for the Earth figures, not for 45 (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): 47 mm/yr computed from Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 (full text, publisher's free PDF, library ForsythUyeda1975.txt) Table 1 (12 plates, area x average absolute speed, Sum A v^2 / Sum A; the table gives average speeds, so the RMS is at least this); 50-100 mm/yr is Zahirovic et al. 2015 section 3.2 ("Global RMS plate velocities have fluctuated between ~5 and 10 cm/yr", accepted manuscript, library Zahirovic2015.txt). The design's "Earth about 40" was the median of plate-samples (4.2 cm/yr, Zahirovic section 3.5), a different statistic. 45 is below both; it is to be calibrated in task 8 against CHK-020; coded 2026-10-02 by force-balance task 5, recorded and not applied: plate_speed_scale_mm_yr in params.rs and every built-in preset; forces.rs Coeffs::new. Its meaning as coded: the speed of a reference oceanic plate (the average starting plate's area, a straight 3,000 km trench of a full-length 80-Myr slab, neighbours at rest), which fixes the basal drag coefficient (FRC-016), not a per-step RMS normalisation Plate speed lane (2026-10-25): still not calibrated against CHK-020. The planet's plates move at 0.55 to 0.6 of it (the motion's RMS 24.9 and 26.4 mm/yr on two planets of `earthlike_quick_16k` at 100,000 samples, the pool's level); the level is linear in it (×1.5 gives ×1.49); Forsyth & Uyeda's Table 6 put on this reference plate gives 65 mm/yr (PLT-575); plates.md "Plate speed (2026-10-25)". Plate speed lane, phase 2 (2026-10-25): not read under PLT-585 (Forsyth & Uyeda's balance): the level follows from Table 6's terminal velocity, 6.43 / 0.89 cm/yr = 72.2 mm/yr (FRC-034), with no speed scale; the switch's off state keeps this row's law. Calibration's first read (2026-10-26, arm sp-v75: `--set tectonics.plate_speed_scale_mm_yr=75` on master 1c2946f2, key 8aaddf52958a, argkey d0cd1773, 8 runs, read against base17-s4, base17's seeds 1 to 4): CHK-020's RMS 28.1 to 42.3 mm/yr (x1.50 for the scale's x1.67, p < 0.001), inside its 40 to 100; CHK-028's plate-steps over 150 mm/yr 0.000 to 0.883 %, past its 0.5 % in 7 of 8 runs; PLT-022's 250 mm/yr guard bound in 3 of 8 runs (4, 2 and 2 steps); the ocean floor's mean age 115.3 to 96.0 Myr and its share older than 180 Myr 0.208 to 0.144 (both p < 0.001; Earth 64 and 0.006). Not applied on master: the guard is not clean (Ben's condition; the coordinator's ruling of 2026-10-26), so master's default stays 45; branch speed-scale-75-parked holds the 75 in params.rs and every preset, with the tests that read the scale. Three of the four plates that bound were consumed plates whose slab pull the force balance still read on their trench of the last resampling (forces.rs does not apply PLT-582); the fourth is a slab plate at the old law's terminal velocity, the scale x 7/3 x the slab's weight; plates.md "Speed scale 75, the calibration's first read (2026-10-26)". Calibrated (2026-10-26, arm fx582-v75: PLT-582's amendment, the force balance's fronts on the current geometry, plus `--set tectonics.plate_speed_scale_mm_yr=75`, on master 77a95f86, key 0fc664149f3b, argkey d0cd1773, 8 runs, read against base18-s4, key 4c55ab83c7a1): CHK-020's RMS 28.5 to 44.4 mm/yr (x1.56 for the scale's x1.67; every run 42.1 to 46.2, p < 0.001), inside its 40 to 100; CHK-028's plate-steps over 150 mm/yr 0.000 to 0.460 % (median), past its 0.5 % in 3 of 8 runs (0.507, 0.558, 0.945; sp-v75 7 of 8); PLT-022's guard bound in none of the 8 runs; the ocean floor's mean age 117.8 to 90.9 Myr and its share older than 180 Myr 0.213 to 0.125 (both p < 0.001; Earth 64 and 0.006). The guard being clean, 75 is the default in params.rs and every built-in preset (the coordinator's ruling of 2026-10-26: the fix and the 75 go on by default together); plates.md "The force balance's front on the current geometry (2026-10-26)". |

Res. Lett. 39:L03305, eq. 1 (after Turcotte & Schubert 1982 and Carlson et al. 1983); Schellart 2004, Geophys. Res. Lett. 31:L07611; Carlson, Hilde & Uyeda 1983, Geophys. Res. Lett. 10:297 | design doc plate-force-balance.md section 3.2 and 12 | `verified` for the law (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): Faccenna et al. 2012 eq. 1, F_sp = 0.25 drho g l 2.32 (kappa A)^(1/2), slab length l times the square root of age (author's preprint, full text, library FaccennaBecker2012.txt); slab pull and slab resistance "an order of magnitude more important than the other forces" (Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 (full text, publisher's free PDF, library ForsythUyeda1975.txt), p. 189, Table 6). Schellart 2004 (net pull 8-12 % of the slab's buoyancy, up to twice ridge push) and Carlson et al. 1983 (speed against age at the trench) abstracts only. The zero below about 10 Myr has no source (`uncited`); coded 2026-10-02 by force-balance task 5, recorded and not applied: forces.rs balance_step and slab_weight (FRC-004, FRC-005); slab_pull in params.rs |
| PLT-183 | force balance | Gross size of slab pull, given for orientation. | docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 3.2, final paragraph | was "slab pull about 1-3 x 10^13 N/m gross"; now "of order 10^13 N/m; 1.4 x 10^13 for 80 Myr floor, 0.7-2.0 x 10^13 for 20-150 Myr" | N/m | Faccenna et al. 2012 eq. 1 (computed); Li et al. 2024 ("10^13 N m^-1", citing Turcotte & Schubert) | design doc plate-force-balance.md section 3.2 | `verified` (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): computed from Faccenna et al. 2012 eq. 1 with their own values (l 700 km, drho 70 kg/m3, g 10 m/s2, kappa 1e-6 m2/s, coefficient 0.25 for the slab's heating; full text, library FaccennaBecker2012.txt); the order agrees with Li et al. 2024 (library Li2024.txt). The coefficient 0.25 makes this a slab pull after heating, not the gross buoyancy (4 times larger); the design's 1-3 x 10^13 "gross" was from memory. Turcotte & Schubert itself not read; coded 2026-10-02 by force-balance task 5, recorded and not applied: forces.rs SLAB_PULL_N_M = 3.0e13 N/m at Earth's gravity (FRC-002): about twice WP-13a's 1.4e13 from Faccenna et al.; kept so that a ridge's push is about 0.1 of the pull (FRC-009); the speeds do not depend on it (the drag is scaled with it, FRC-016), the stresses and the ridge-push ratio do |
| PLT-184 | force balance | Slab length grows with convergence up to the depth limit; a slab breaks off after continental crust has been in the trench this long. | docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 3.3 and 6 | slab_depth_km 700; slab_breakoff_myr 10 | km, Myr | van Hunen & Allen 2011, EPSL 302:27-37 (10-25 Myr); Faccenna et al. 2012 (700 km slab); van Hunen, Faccenna & Allen 2011, EGU2011-6298 | design doc plate-force-balance.md section 3.3 and 12 | `abstract-only` (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): 700 is a slab length, as Faccenna et al. 2012 take every slab ("assumed constant at 700 km considering the present mean slab dip of 55 deg", full text), not the depth of the upper mantle, which ends near 660 km; break-off 20-25 Myr after collision onset for an old, strong slab and 10 Myr for a young or weak one (van Hunen, Faccenna & Allen 2011, EGU General Assembly abstract EGU2011-6298, read; library vanHunen2011EGU.txt); the EPSL paper's text could not be obtained. The default 10 is the young-slab end; coded 2026-10-02 by force-balance task 5, recorded and not applied: forces.rs grow_slab (FRC-019, FRC-020); slab_depth_km, slab_breakoff_myr in params.rs. The EPSL paper's full text was read 2026-10-20 (bibliography vanHunenAllen2011): break-off "at 20–25 Myr after the onset of continental collision" for old slabs, "already 10 Myr after" for young ones, as the abstract had it |
| PLT-185 | force balance | Slab resistance: resistance to sinking proportional to speed towards the trench; gives a terminal velocity and replaces the speed cap. | docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 3.2 and 6 | slab_resistance 3 (times the basal drag of an average starting plate) | relative | Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 (terminal velocity) | design doc plate-force-balance.md | `calibrated` (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): the principle is Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 (full text, publisher's free PDF, library ForsythUyeda1975.txt), p. 178: slab-attached plates move at "6-9 cm/year", "effectively the terminal velocity of the dense slab falling in a viscous medium", and their solution's terminal velocity is 7.0 cm/yr (p. 189). | PLT-186 | force balance | Slab suction on the overriding plate as a share of slab pull; with pull it explains why subducting plates move about four times faster than the others. | docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 3.2 and 6 | slab_suction_share 0.3 | share | Conrad & Lithgow-Bertelloni 2002, Science 298:207; 2004, J. Geophys. Res. 109:B10407 (from abstracts) | design doc plate-force-balance.md | `abstract-only`; WP-13a read both abstracts again (full texts not obtainable): 2004 says subducting plates "currently move ~4 times faster than nonsubducting plates" and suction and pull are "about 40 and 60 %" of the forces on plates, suction "only ~30 %" with a low-viscosity asthenosphere; 2002 says upper-mantle slab pull is "about half of the total driving force". Their suction drives both plates; the design applies it to the overriding plate only, so 0.3 is not their quantity. Their suction / pull is 0.43-0.67. The 0.3 is the open-loop test's value; question for Ben in STATUS.md; coded 2026-10-02 by force-balance task 5, recorded and not applied: forces.rs balance_step (Drive::Suction), on the overriding plate only. Since 2026-10-24 the share is the suction at rest of a law that saturates at the slab's terminal velocity (PLT-540, `forces::Balance::add_suction`); the fixed share is the switch's off state Plate speed lane, phase 2 (2026-10-25): under PLT-585 the share is Table 6's 0.50 / 6.43 = 0.078 of the pull, independent of speed ("we modelled it to be velocity independent and perpendicular to the trench", pp. 170-171; FRC-033, FRC-034); slab_suction_share is not read. On the overriding oceanic plates it is the term the guard binds on (plates.md "Plate speed, phase 2"). |

Geophys. 36:27 (ridge push under 10 percent of the driving force); Copley et al. 2010; Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 | design doc plate-force-balance.md section 3.2 | `verified` for the ratio (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): slabs give "in excess of 90 %" of the driving force and lithospheric thickening "of the order of 10 %" (Lithgow-Bertelloni & Richards 1998, abstract and conclusions, full text, UCL Discovery, library LithgowBertelloniRichards1998.txt); per unit length ridge push 0.36 against slab pull 6.43 (Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 (full text, publisher's free PDF, library ForsythUyeda1975.txt), Table 6): 0.06. Absolute sizes from abstracts: ridge push ~2.5 x 10^12 N/m and the Himalaya-Tibet's resistance ~5-6 x 10^12 N/m (Copley et al. 2010, abstract); Molnar & Lyon-Caen's ~7 x 10^12 only as cited by Schmalholz & Mancktelow 2016 (full text); Parsons & Richter not read. Res. Lett. 33:L05312; Forsyth & Uyeda 1975; Coltice et al. 2019, Sci. Adv. 5:eaax4295 | design doc plate-force-balance.md section 3.2 | `verified` (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): continental roots "increase traction magnitudes by a factor of 2-5 compared to 100 km thick lithosphere" (Conrad & Lithgow-Bertelloni 2006, abstract and section 3, full text, UCL Discovery, library ConradLB2006.txt); the factor of 8 is in Forsyth & Uyeda's body: "Drag under the continents ... is approximately eight times as strong as the mantle drag under the oceanic portion of the plates" (p. 189; Table 6 gives 5.65 +/- 2.22 against 0.82 +/- 0.30) and the viscosity under continents "about eight times larger" (p. 194). 3 lies inside the first and below the second (was `from-memory`); coded 2026-10-02 by force-balance task 5, recorded and not applied: forces.rs Balance::area_into (keel factor on continental samples) Plate speed lane, phase 2 (2026-10-25): under PLT-585 the keel factor is Table 6's (0.82 + 5.65) / 0.82 = 7.89 (FRC-034), Forsyth & Uyeda's "approximately eight times" (p. 189); keel_drag is not read. |
| PLT-189 | force balance | Resistance along plate boundaries, proportional to relative speed; ties small plates to their neighbours. | docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 3.2 and 6 | boundary_resistance 3 (times basal drag of an average starting plate); chosen by open-loop test (1-3 percent of plate-steps above 150 mm/yr) | relative | Forsyth & Uyeda 1975 (transform resistance) | design doc plate-force-balance.md section 4 | `calibrated` (WP-13a, 2026-10-02): the term is Forsyth & Uyeda's transform-fault resistance (Table 6, 0.36 per unit length; p. 178: no relation of speed to transform length, so it is "not strong enough to be the primary control"; full text); the 3 was chosen in the open-loop test against the fast-plate target CHK-028 (was `abstract-only`); coded 2026-10-02 by force-balance task 5, recorded and not applied: forces.rs Coeffs::new (boundary_res, FRC-017), against the real neighbour's motion, half from each side Plate speed lane (2026-10-25): Forsyth & Uyeda's transform resistance (Table 6, read again: 0.36 per km, "opp. rel. motion", per unit length and not per unit velocity) does not depend on speed and acts on transform faults only, and they have no ridge resistance; the coded term is viscous and acts on every boundary, ridges and trenches included, so the citation names the term's place, not its law. After the slab resistance it holds the planet's plates most: off with c_b kept, the RMS 45.8 and 48.7 mm/yr against 27.3 and 29.3 in one step's balance (plates.md "Plate speed (2026-10-25)"). Plate speed lane, phase 2 (2026-10-25): not read under PLT-585: no viscous resistance on any boundary. Forsyth & Uyeda's transform (0.36) and colliding (0.16) resistances are speed-independent friction on those boundaries only, by their 60 degree rule (FRC-035); boundary_resistance is not read. The switch's off state keeps this row's law. |

It is an oceanic (island) arc cell when its crust is oceanic or its arc magma is mostly of the oceanic accumulator (arc_oceanic_m above arc_continental_m: an island arc that emerged and matured into continental crust still counts as oceanic), otherwise a continental arc cell. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/dynamics.rs: arc_stats, is_island_arc | rule | rule | none cited (a definition) | build log 2026-10-02 | `game-choice` |
| PLT-221 | plate-check targets | Boundary length by kind (the record's mix_km): one sample spacing per boundary sample. The motion towards each neighbouring plate is judged across the boundary: the relative velocity's component along the boundary's normal (PLT-310), zero where it lies within 20 degrees of the boundary (a transform, PLT-311). A front (a sample on the overriding side of an active subduction contact whose boundary with the subducting plate converges) counts a whole spacing, once per front, its class by the crust of its fastest-converging neighbour there; a sample with a neighbour across a parting boundary counts half a spacing from each side; the subducting side of a front is not counted; the rest (transform, or converging without a contact yet) counts half a spacing from each side. Ocean-ocean, ocean-continent and collision are by the crust of the two samples at the front; since 2026-10-21 an ocean-ocean front whose overriding plate has continental crust within the arc's distance counts as ocean-continent (PLT-370). Taken right after the fronts are found. Until 2026-10-20 the motion was judged along single lattice edges (the fronts' own test): a transform's zigzag of edges converges along half of them and parts along the rest, so the model read almost no transform (3 % against Bird's 28 %) and counted it as fronts and ridges. Since 2026-10-20 the arcs' fronts take the same test (PLT-330). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/dynamics.rs: classify_boundary, boundary_mix, Neighbour::normal | rule | km | none cited (a definition; the transform test is Bird's, PLT-311) | build log 2026-10-02; build log 2026-10-20 "Land shape" | `game-choice` |
| PLT-222 | collisions | Convergence across a collision front: the flux of the subducting plate's velocity relative to the overriding plate through the front, over the front's length. The front is the chain of Voronoi edges between the overriding plate's edge samples and their lattice neighbours of other plates; each lattice edge contributes its Voronoi edge's length (cotangent formula over the two triangles on the edge) times the inward component of the relative velocity along it. The front's length is the length of the summed (length x outward direction) vector, and at least half the summed edge length. Plates sliding past each other give zero, a parting contact a negative value. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/shortening.rs: fronts, voronoi_edge_rad, normal_convergence, Front::length_km, Front::convergence_mm_yr | conv = sum_e l_e (-(v_under - v_over) . n_e) / max(abs(sum_e l_e n_e), 0.5 sum_e l_e); l_e = 0.5 d_e (cot a + cot b) | mm/yr | none cited: geometry (the flux of a velocity field through a polyline equals its flux through the straight line between the polyline's ends when the field has no divergence, as a rigid rotation has not). Tests: head-on within 2 percent of the analytic mean, pure strike-slip under 2 percent of the speed. | code comment (module docs of shortening.rs); build log 2026-10-02 (plate-motion task 2) | `uncited` |

It begins on the first step the contact is live (overlap seen this step), its front has at least 3 samples (PLT-057's count) and converges at 10 mm/yr or more (PLT-058's confirmation rate: a slower front only touches; counted from above zero, the onset convergence was a few mm/yr). Each later step with a front adds max(convergence, 0) x dt to its shortening and that times the front's length to the crust that crossed the front. It ends in a transfer or weld, a parting (PLT-225), a polarity reversal, or the contact's close. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/shortening.rs: step_collision, advance; plates/state.rs: Collision | shortening_km += max(conv, 0) x dt_myr; area_km2 += that x front_km | km; km2 | design docs/design/plate-collisions-and-welding.md section 4.2 (2A and the entry rule of 2B) and section 7 task 2; docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 9 task 2 | code comment; build log 2026-10-02 (plate-motion task 2) | `game-choice` |

At the model's collisions about 40 km of crust crosses per Myr at 40 mm/yr, about 1,600 km3 per km of front per Myr, so the deep loss is about 3 % and all suture losses about 7 % of what crosses: a retained share of 0.93-0.97, 0.95 taken (read by this agent, 2026-10-05). Recomputed 2026-10-21 (the crust flux into collision fronts, PLT-401) on the crossing the model measures, 17.1 mm/yr on 38.3 km of crust, 655 km3 per km of front per Myr: Stern & Scholl's 50 is 7.6 % of it and 65 with the sediment 9.9 %; the model loses 5 % by the share and 6.6 % for want of room in the belt, 11.9 % in all, so 0.95 is in line. Ingalls et al. 2016 (the abstract) find about half of India's crust missing from the India-Asia mass balance; van Hinsbergen et al. 2017 dispute it, Yakovlev & Clark 2014 conserve it on a 23 to 29 km margin, and Stern & Scholl take 10 % for India as a minimum: one collision's estimate, not a global share. | code comment; build log 2026-10-02 (plate tasks 3 and 4); build log 2026-10-05 (force balance tasks 7-8; the coordinator's choice under Ben's validation policy of that day); plates.md "The crust flux into collision fronts (2026-10-21)", "PLT-242 against its sources" | `verified` (Stern & Scholl 2010 p. 18-19 read again in the library 2026-10-21; the value unchanged. ### orogenic collapse and the thickest crust (added 2026-10-03; Ben, decisions log 2026-10-03: "Merge, then give area back")

| PLT-254 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Orogenic collapse: each step a visible continental sample that no orogeny has raised for the quiet time (PLT-259) and whose crust is thicker than T_c (PLT-256) releases q = (1 - exp(-dt / tau)) x (T - T_c) of its thickness (tau PLT-255), its own rock mix thinned in proportion (pure shear); the crust released fills the nearest samples of the same plate thinner than the normal column T_n (35.1 km, pools::continental_column), within the reach (PLT-257), up to T_n, nearest first (thinned continental crust, then the plate's own ocean floor; not trench samples); an ocean sample filled to T_n becomes continental. Crust with no room within reach is not released. Volume is conserved. Heights follow Airy within the column's Airy height z_A(T) = z_0 + (T - T_n) / 7.0 - (the pull of its eclogite root, PLT-282) (z_0 the middle of continent_elevation_m; 5.5 and no root before 2026-10-06, PLT-281): a source sinks by its isostatic change but not below z_A of what it keeps, a receiver rises by g / 5.5 but not above z_A of its new column. The driver is thickness, not height: in the coupled pipeline the surface stage owns the elevation, and stacked or overlapped crust sits under ground that does not show it (at 500 Myr on master 6f72f0dd a fifth of the continental samples are thicker than 70 km at a median 200-500 m). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/collapse.rs: step | rule | rule | Dewey 1988 (Tectonics 7:1123, abstract: collapse explains "the return of orogenic large crustal thicknesses to normal without very much erosional denudation"); Wernicke et al. 1987 (GSL Spec. Publ. 28:203, full text: "the locus of extension is controlled principally by crustal thickness"); Jolivet & Brun 2010 (full text: the extended Aegean crust "around 26 km"). Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-03. The transport to the nearest room is the model's, not a flow solve. | code comment; build log 2026-10-03 (orogenic collapse) | `abstract-only` |
| PLT-255 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Collapse e-folding time tau of the excess thickness. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.collapse_myr; plates/collapse.rs: step | 15.0 (default and every built-in preset; 0 turns the collapse off; 0 on the 4k test planet `planet_world_4k`, whose GameTest fixtures keep master's planet, build log 2026-10-04) | Myr | Hillenbrand & Williams 2022 (GRL 49:e2022GL100435, abstract): Variscan "thick crust (55–70 km) ... developed ... between ca. 350 and 315 Ma. Crustal thinning occurred from ca. 315 to 290 Ma" (25 Myr, about two e-foldings at 15 Myr); Bird 1991 (JGR 96:10275, abstract): the Basin and Range "cannot maintain short-wavelength (100 km) Moho relief of more than 1 km for times greater than 10–20 m.y. at most"; Caledonides: collapse from about 405 Ma, detachments active 400-365 Ma (Peron-Pinvidic & Osmundsen 2020, full text). Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-03. | code comment; build log 2026-10-03 (orogenic collapse) | `abstract-only` |
| PLT-256 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Collapse threshold T_c: quiet crust thicker than this thins back to it; thinner crust keeps its root. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.collapse_thickness_km; plates/collapse.rs: step | 50.0 (default and every built-in preset since 2026-10-05, was 55.0; at least T_n) | km | The Urals keep "a 50–56 km crustal thickness beneath the central part of the orogen" as an unextended orogen, about 300 Myr after their collision (Carbonell et al. 2000, JGR 105:13755, abstract); the Variscan crust that collapsed was 55-70 km (Hillenbrand & Williams 2022, abstract); "Isostatic forces uplift crust thicker than about 50 km, leading to erosion and crustal thinning" (Mooney, Prodehl & Pavlenkova 2002, IASPEI handbook, USGS author copy, full text). 55 km is between the thickest root that stays and the thinnest crust that collapsed. Chosen among 50 and 55 by the 1000 Myr crust share (50 with the quiet time: 0.52 at the end of seed 1, over the 0.45 ceiling; 55: 0.41-0.42); within the sources' range, so the pick is a calibration. Set to 50 with the switch to the force balance (plate task 8, 2026-10-05), under Ben's validation policy of that day (physics from sources; land and crust share are reported, not tuned): 50 km is the figure the sources state (Mooney et al.'s "about 50 km"), where 55 was a pick between sources made for the crust share. Measured beside it (build log 2026-10-05): on four paired 500 Myr runs 50 gave a higher crust share and more land than 55, and 45 a lower crust share. Comparison: orogens average 46.3 km, the continents 41.1 km (Christensen & Mooney 1995, abstract). Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-03. | code comment; build log 2026-10-03 (orogenic collapse) | `cited-unverified` |
| PLT-257 | crust and isostasy (plates) | How far from a collapsing column its crust may go. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.collapse_reach_km; plates/collapse.rs: step | 5,000 (the default and every built-in preset since 2026-10-23, a plate's size, so the crust a collapse releases reaches its continent's edge; 200.0 before); at least 1.5 sample spacings (REACH_MIN_SPACINGS: on a coarse lattice, such as the 4k test planet, 200 km is shorter than one spacing and nothing would move) | km (Earth-normalized) | Rey, Teyssier & Whitney 2010 (Lithosphere 2:328, full text): "Over 15 m.y., the length scale of channel-flow extrusion is 150 km rather than 1500 km"; Caledonian detachments displaced "40–100 km" (Peron-Pinvidic & Osmundsen 2020, full text); the Cordilleran extension "widened to several hundred kilometres" (Wernicke et al. 1987, full text). 200 km is about three samples of the quick preset. Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-03. Those lengths are a lower-crustal channel's and a detachment's; the whole lithosphere spreads over the orogen's width or more (England & Houseman 1986, physics-gap-audit.md gap 1), which the default's 5,000 km stands in for until a flow solve replaces the reach | code comment; build log 2026-10-03 (orogenic collapse); plates.md "On by default (2026-10-23)" | `cited-unverified` (the 200 km; 5,000 km since 2026-10-23 is the coordinator's call under Ben's ship rule, measured with the sheet: no reach limit within a plate) |
| PLT-258 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The thickest continental crust: every step a visible continental column thicker than this loses the excess that finds no room to spread (PLT-277, since 2026-10-05) from the bottom of the column (mafic pools first, then felsic plutonic, metamorphic, felsic volcanic, sedimentary, sediment) to the mantle; it gives no area. Stands for continental subduction and the foundering of an eclogitic root, which the model's stacking (belt cap by height PLT-253, overlap accretion at resampling PLT-228) does not have. Since 2026-10-18 crust that passes a column's step ceiling (PLT-287) and finds no room goes down the same way, whatever the column's thickness. Since 2026-10-23 (stage 3 of the lithosphere's thermal state, PLT-477, on by default; the all-at-once removal and its switch `root_removal` are gone) a column sinks 1 − e^(−δt / 10 Myr) of its no-room excess over the cap per step rather than all of it, the pace of a Rayleigh-Taylor drop-off. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.crust_max_km; plates/collapse.rs: founder | 70.0 (default and every built-in preset; 0 = no limit; 0 on the 4k test planet `planet_world_4k`, as PLT-255) | km | Mooney, Prodehl & Pavlenkova 2002 (full text): "The thickest crust (70+ km) occurs beneath the Tibetan Plateau and the South American Andes"; Wang et al. 2021 (Nat. Commun. 12:1069, full text): "Moho at 80 ± 2 km" in the Lhasa terrane; Zhao et al. 2020 (G-cubed 21:e2019GC008849, full text): 60–80 km within the plateau, at most about 79 km; Stern & Scholl 2010 (Int. Geol. Rev. 52:1, full text): "the bulk of the approximately 40 km-thick crust that must have originally existed is missing" at UHP terranes, and continental crust lost at collision zones about 0.7 km3/yr (Scholl & von Huene 2009, as cited). Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-03. Scholl & von Huene 2009 (Geol. Soc. London Spec. Publ. 318:105-125, abstract read through Crossref, 2026-10-18; the paper is paywalled): at ocean-margin subduction zones "crustal recycling ... is assessed at c. 60 km3 Ma−1 km−1 (c. 60% by subduction erosion). The corresponding global recycling rate is c. 2.5 AU"; at crust-suturing (collision) zones "the combined upper and lower plate losses of CIA crust via subduction erosion, sediment subduction, and lower plate crustal detachment and sinking are assessed far less securely at c. 115 km3 Ma−1 km−1. At a global length of 6000 km, recycling at CSSZs is accordingly c. 0.7 AU" (the 6000 km is the assumed past mean, "one-half that under way since the early Tertiary"); "The collective loss of CIA crust ... is thus estimated at c. 3.2 AU", against 2.7 AU added (1 AU = 1 km3 a−1 of solid material). The 0.7 km3/yr is the paper's figure for collision zones only, as Stern & Scholl cite it; at today's collision length (twice 6000 km, the agent's arithmetic) it would be about 1.4 km3/yr, and the paper's loss at all subduction zones is 3.2 km3/yr (build log 2026-10-18 "Resampling lumps"). The removal order (bottom first) is the model's. | code comment; build log 2026-10-03 (orogenic collapse) | `cited-unverified` (Scholl & von Huene 2009: `abstract-only`, 2026-10-18) |
| PLT-259 | crust and isostasy (plates) | A sample collapses only once no orogeny has raised it for this long (its orogeny stamp, PLT-247 and the subduction uplift's, older than this; at least the current step): the convergence holds a range up, and collapse follows the end of the thickening. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.collapse_quiet_myr; plates/collapse.rs: step | 10.0 (default and every built-in preset) | Myr | Hillenbrand & Williams 2022 (abstract): Variscan thickening "between ca. 350 and 315 Ma", thinning "from ca. 315 to 290 Ma" (thinning began as thickening ended); Basler et al. 2026 (Tectonics 45, abstract, read by the research sub-agent): "most crustal thinning was contemporaneous with a change in plate boundary conditions"; Sundell et al. 2021 (GSA Today 31(6), full text): Tibet's Miocene east-west extension came without net thinning while convergence went on. 10 Myr is the model's own stall time before a weld (PLT-246), when the plates stop converging. | code comment; build log 2026-10-03 (orogenic collapse) | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-03; the sources say when, not how long) |

### the ocean–continent transition of rifted margins (added 2026-10-04; Ben, decisions log 2026-10-03: "Merge both, then fix the cause")

| PLT-261 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Rifted-margin taper: each step, the samples of a plate within W/2 of its own ocean–continent boundary (an edge between a visible continental and a visible oceanic sample of the plate; none at a trench, in a subduction zone or on an arc, no continental sample an orogeny raised within the quiet time PLT-259, and no oceanic sample with more crystalline crust than T_n: the plate model keeps stacked and accreted crust under the oceanic label) get a signed distance d from the boundary on the planet (positive inland, the boundary half a sample spacing beyond each boundary sample) and a target crystalline thickness T*(d) = T_o + (T_n - T_o) S(1/2 + d/W) (S is PLT-475's smoothstep since 2026-10-23; the linear clamp(1/2 + d/W, 0, 1) before), T_n = 33.0 km (the normal continental column less its sediment and sedimentary rock), T_o = 7 km (the ocean's). Within each stretch of margin (boundary samples grouped round seeds at least W/2 apart, every zone sample with the seed of its nearest boundary sample through its own crust) the samples thicker than T* give (1 - exp(-dt / tau)) of what would bring the stretch to the profile to the samples thinner than T*, their own rock thinned in proportion (pure shear); the sinks take the sources' rock (with `transition_ocean_crust`, off by default, an oceanic sink takes the same volume as ocean crust, PLT-294); volume is conserved and crust types do not change (the thickened ocean-side crust stays oceanic, so continental area and the crust share are not redefined). The profile (beta = T_n / T from 1 at the hinge to T_n / T_o at the toe) is centred on the model's boundary and symmetric about it, so stretching the model's one-sample step into it keeps the crust's volume. Since 2026-10-23 (stage 2 of the lithosphere's thermal state, on by default; the linear ramp and its switch `transition_beta` are gone) the profile is PLT-475's smoothstep and the sources' lithosphere stretches with their crust (PLT-476). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/transition.rs: step; plates/mod.rs (after collapse, before the surface step) | rule | rule | McKenzie 1978 (EPSL 40:25, scanned full text: "a unit length of continental lithosphere is suddenly extended to a length beta"; the subsidence "depend[s] only on the amount of stretching"); Sapin, Ringenbach & Clerc 2021 (Sci. Rep. 11:8199, full text: in the Coral Sea "a thinning of the crust from 22 km down to a 7–8 km thick ... in direct contact with the steady oceanic crust of similar thickness"); Brune et al. 2014 (Nat. Commun. 5:4014, full text: hyper-extended crust "<10 km" thick over 70-200 km). Comparison: passive margins are 105,000 km of today's plate edges against 53,000 km convergent (Bradley 2008, full text). Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-04. The profile's form (PLT-475), the catchments and the transport within a stretch are the model's. | code comment; build log 2026-10-04 (ocean–continent transition) | `cited-unverified` |
| PLT-262 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The transition's width W, km on the Earth-sized model sphere (R_model, `tectonics.model_radius_km`, 6,370 km), Earth-normalized like the plate model's other lengths (PLT-003), so the planet's radius does not enter: the taper is 200/6,370 rad wide on every planet, 24.5 km on the quick 16k planet's 782 km radius, and 2.8 L0 samples across there (71 km spacing on the model sphere; 4.4 at the full presets' 250,000 samples). Until 2026-10-22 W was km on the planet (divided by the planet's radius): 1,632 km on the model sphere on the quick 16k planet, eight times Earth's margins. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.transition_width_km; plates/transition.rs: width_rad | 200.0 (default and every built-in preset; 0 = off, as in planet_world_4k) | km (model sphere) | Franke 2013 (Mar. Petrol. Geol. 43:63, accepted manuscript, full text): at volcanic margins, "the majority of passive continental margins worldwide", "Crustal thinning typically occurs over a comparably short distance in the order of only 50 to 100 km", while "Magma-poor rifted margins in contrast show wide domains of extended crust"; Sapin et al. 2021 (full text): the necking domain from "~ 50 km" to "more than 110 km across the shelf", the exhumation domain "more than 150 km"; Brune et al. 2014 (full text): hyper-extended slivers "between 70 km offshore Iberia and 200 km off Angola"; Harris et al. 2014 (Mar. Geol. 352:4, author copy, full text): passive-margin shelves 88 km and slopes 46 km wide on average. 200 km is inside the sources' 50-300 km and about Harris's shelf plus slope (134 km) with the rise. Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-04. | code comment; build log 2026-10-04 (ocean–continent transition) | `cited-unverified`; measured 2026-10-04 (build log, ocean–continent transition): passive slopes 500-2,000 m 4.5 to 2.6 deg (100 km: 3.0), land at 500 Myr 0.235 to 0.184 (100 km: 0.182, 0.202 over 12 runs); the land cost is in proportion to W, about 8x Earth's share of the continents on the quick 16k planet; measured again 2026-10-22 on master df40ce80 (sea-level.md, "The continents under the sea and the sea's height at the end", 12 runs an arm): W 24.5 km (200 km on the model sphere) takes the continental crust under the sea 0.414 to 0.279 (p 0.002), land 0.247 to 0.274 (p 0.002), the largest landmass 0.104 to 0.125 (p 0.073), the passive slopes 2.5 to 3.2 deg; W 70 km 0.384 (p 0.40), 0.253, 0.080, 2.8 deg (those arms on the code where W was km on the planet, so 24.5 km stood for 200 km on the model sphere); the value 200 is Ben's (2026-10-05) and its meaning moved to the model sphere on 2026-10-22 (decisions log, D6 "200 km on sphere"); measured on the shipped change (plates.md and sea-level.md, "The transition's width and the drowned continents", "W on the model sphere, in the code"; master 10bafdae against this change with PLT-400 on, 12 runs an arm, medians, two-sided Mann-Whitney U over the runs): continental crust under the sea 0.414 to 0.278 (p < 0.001, all 12 runs fall), land 0.247 to 0.300 (p 0.001), the largest landmass 0.104 to 0.124 (p 0.34), land above 2 km 0.248 to 0.271 (p 0.80), coast with high ground 0.369 to 0.411 (p 0.033), land under ice 0.170 to 0.170 (p 0.93), collisions 1.65 to 1.80 per 100 Myr (p 0.84), criteria met per run 1 to 2 (p 0.018), passive slopes 500 to 2,000 m 2.5 to 3.1 deg |
| PLT-263 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The e-folding time tau towards the taper. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.transition_myr; plates/transition.rs: step | 20.0 (default, every built-in preset and planet_world_4k; 0 = off) | Myr | none for the value: a rift stretches its crust for tens of Myr before break-up, and McKenzie 1978's thermal subsidence (tau = 62.8 Myr, full text) is carried by the ocean's own age-depth law (PLT-264), not by this time. 20 Myr is ten 2 Myr steps: fast enough that a margin opened by a rift has its taper before its ocean is old, slow enough not to chase every resampling. | code comment; build log 2026-10-04 (ocean–continent transition) | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-04) |
| PLT-264 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Heights follow the crust moved along the line through two reference columns, a continent of T_n at z_0 (the middle of continent_elevation_m) and old ocean floor of T_o at z_f (oceanic_floor_elevation_m): a metre of crust moved changes the height by 1/f, f = (T_n - T_o) / (z_0 - z_f), on both sides, so the two sides of the boundary meet. An oceanic sample of the zone then cools (the dampening, PLT-129) towards a held floor z_f + (min(T, T*) - T_o) / f instead of z_f (bounded by its target, so at most about ridge depth): a young margin stands near ridge depth and the taper deepens as its ocean ages (the ocean's age-depth law stands for McKenzie's thermal subsidence). Since plate task 8 (2026-10-17; the gap budget's recommendation 3) every other oceanic sample cools towards the floor its own crust holds up on the same line, capped at the half-way column's crystalline thickness (T_n + T_o) / 2 = 20 km (about -2,540 m on the presets' line), and an island arc stands up by the crust it adds on the same line (PLT-045): with arcs raised by their thickness, the bare floor would have sunk them and their crust (about 2 km of the 40-55 km crust's gap, the gap budget). The ocean's own 7 km cools to the bare floor as before. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/transition.rs: crust_per_metre, held_floor_m; plates/mod.rs: surface (held_cap); plates/pools.rs: surface_step; plates/subduction.rs: fronts_and_uplift (island arcs); test thick_ocean_crust_cools_towards_the_floor_it_holds_up | f = 4.24 with the presets' 525 m and -5,600 m (between the model's dry and water-loaded Airy factors, 5.5 and 3.78); cap 20 km of crystalline crust | m of crust per m of height | McKenzie 1978 (full text): initial subsidence of stretched crust is isostatic, thermal subsidence follows (Table 1: rho_c 2.8, rho_m 3.33, rho_w 1.0 g/cm3).
