### resampling and initiation fixes (added 2026-10-02 with the continental crust fix)

| PLT-228 | ridges and ocean crust | At a resampling each rigid group's extent is its footprint: the cells of the old lattice that its samples owned at the last resampling (each lattice triangle split between its corners by the largest barycentric weight), moved rigidly with the group. A new point inside the group's own triangles interpolates them (Cortial); on the group's boundary strip (a triangle shared with another group) it interpolates the group's own corners; a point in no footprint is a gap and becomes new ocean floor (PLT-100). Where footprints overlap the top crust is the visible one, then the overriding plate of a subduction contact, then continental, then higher, then the lower group id; hidden crust, the same plate's and the subducting plate's crust give their conserved pools to it (PLT-104). Replaces whole-triangle covers plus, for uncovered points, a copy of the nearest visible sample within 0.75 spacings: the subducting plate's crust under an overriding plate's boundary strip covered the strip, so the overriding margin lost its outer half-spacing at every resampling (about 400-800 visible continental samples of 100,000 per resampling late in a 1000 Myr run). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/ridges.rs: footprint, resample | rule; test resampling_conserves_continental_area: continental area through one resampling within 0.2 % on a static planet and with an ocean run 0.1 rad under a continent (old rule -0.35 %, this rule -0.03 %, mean of six lattices on 20,000 samples) | rule | none (a numerical method; conserving area through a change of lattice is the requirement) | build log 2026-10-02 "Resampling crust loss" | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-02) |
| PLT-229 | subduction | A subduction initiation that would split off a plate at least half continental is not made (the split's continental samples are slivers stranded in the old floor, PLT-067; a continent cut off by the old floor went to the new plate whole in one test history). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/rifting.rs: initiate | no initiation when 2 x continental samples >= samples of the split | rule | none (initiation splits old oceanic lithosphere: PLT-062, PLT-067) | build log 2026-10-02 "Resampling crust loss" | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-02) |

### the switch to the force balance (added 2026-10-04, force-balance tasks 7 and 8)

| PLT-270 | subduction | A subduction initiation plants a slab under the fracture on the old floor it splits off (the new plate keeps its parent's motion); the slab hangs and pulls the new plate towards its parent until the trench forms, then grows by the convergence. Since 2026-10-25 (PLT-655, on by default) the slab hangs from the older side of the fracture, the split's or the parent's, and the trench that forms takes its polarity. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.initiation_slab_km; plates/rifting.rs: initiate | 150.0 (default and every preset) | km | Gurnis, Hall & Lavier 2004, Geochem. Geophys. Geosyst. 5:Q07001 (full text, read 2026-10-21; re-read 2026-10-23, V1 gap 2): "A FZ [fracture zone] must be underthrust by the older plate for 100-150 km before a transition from forced to self-sustaining states is reached" | code comment; design docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 3.3 ("It plants a slab of a starting length on the old floor") | `verified` (2026-10-23: was `from-memory`; the 150 km is the top of Gurnis et al.'s range, read) |
| PLT-271 | subduction | A slab that no oceanic front feeds this step hangs: it pulls at the subducting plate's samples beside the overriding plate (with suction and slab resistance as at a front) and is gone after `slab_breakoff_myr` without a front. Slabs are known by their two plates, so a contact that flickers off and on keeps its slab (task 5: slab restarts were the main source of the balance's noise). A split's latent copy of a slab (PLT-534) does not hang. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/forces.rs: balance_step (hang); plates/state.rs: SlabTrack | idle limit = slab_breakoff_myr (10) | Myr | none | code comment; build log 2026-10-04 | `game-choice` (agent) |
| PLT-272 | rifting | A rift that did not open heals: two plates a rift separated whose relative speed has stayed under `weld_speed_mm_yr` along their whole shared boundary for `weld_after_myr` (and that overlap nowhere) become one again; the larger keeps its id, the motion is the area-weighted mean. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/rifting.rs: heal | weld_speed_mm_yr 5; weld_after_myr 10 (PLT-246) | mm/yr, Myr | none (definition: "Two plates are one plate when they have stopped moving relative to each other", design section 1) | design docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 3.4 ("The same rule merges the two sides of a rift that did not open") | `game-choice` (design) |
| PLT-273 | plate setup | Plates start at rest. The starting ocean floor's ages come from an earlier motion drawn for the purpose (a random pole, PLT-026, and a speed of 0.5-1.5 times `plate_speed_scale_mm_yr` at the centroid), which is not kept: the floor is aged by its distance from the boundaries that diverge under it. Under `ocean_depth: gdh1` the distance gives the ages' order and PLT-356 their spread (2026-10-21). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/init.rs: initial_state | speed 0.5-1.5 x 45 mm/yr | mm/yr | none | code comment; design docs/design/plate-force-balance.md section 6 ("plates start at rest plus the mantle flow") | `game-choice` (agent) |
| PLT-274 | plate setup | The slabs of the earlier motion: wherever the earlier motion that ages the starting sea floor (PLT-273) converged across a plate boundary with ocean floor on its sinking side (at least 3 converging boundary samples), a slab of full length hangs from that boundary at the start; the sinking side follows a new contact's rule (ocean floor under continent, the older floor where both are oceanic, none where both are continental). Plates still start at rest; the balance sets them moving within the first relaxation time, and a slab whose trench never forms is gone after `slab_breakoff_myr` (PLT-271). Without it a planet started at rest has no slab for its first 40-60 Myr (no arc volcano in a 40 Myr test run). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/init.rs: earlier_slabs | km = slab_depth_km (700) | km | none (Earth always has slabs: about 51,000 km of subduction boundary today, Bird 2003, PLT-213) | code comment; build log 2026-10-04 | `game-choice` (agent; question in STATUS.md) |
| PLT-275 | force balance | The strength of the collision's buoyancy (FRC-030), relative to its physical size: the buoyancy of the continental lithosphere a collision has dragged down resists the convergence beside the belt's weight. Added with the switch to the force balance (plate task 7) because the belt's weight alone did not stop collisions of plates that slabs elsewhere pull. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: collision_buoyancy; every built-in preset | 1 | relative | Cloos 1993 (abstract, as FRC-028) | code comment; plate-forces.md | `game-choice` (agent, build log 2026-10-05; 1 = the physical size) |
| PLT-276 | collisions | Continental crust that a collision hides under the overriding plate (a contact with a collision state, `subduction::resolve` marks it `Sample::stacked` each step) is consumed at a resampling, not accreted to the column on top: the belt and the wedge already stacked `collision_stacking_share` of its volume and the rest went down with the slab (PLT-242). Until 2026-10-05 its conserved pools were also accreted (PLT-228), so a collision put up to 1.8 times the crossed volume into the overriding plate, which the thickest-crust limit (PLT-258) then removed. Other hidden crust (forced-subducted continent outside a collision, trench sediment) still accretes. Since 2026-10-18 the mark stays while the crust stays hidden, through a transfer or a weld (PLT-291), and the colliding plate's crust still visible inside the overriding plate's footprint is consumed as well (PLT-292). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/subduction.rs: resolve; plates/ridges.rs: resample; plates/state.rs: Sample.stacked | rule; tests a_resampling_accretes_hidden_continent_once, a_resampling_consumes_the_visible_crust_a_collision_took, crossed_crust_stays_under_through_a_transfer_and_a_weld | rule | none (bookkeeping: each volume counted once) | build log 2026-10-05 (plate task 7) | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-05) |
| PLT-277 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Continental crust thicker than `crust_max_km` (PLT-258) first spreads the excess at once to the nearest room on its plate within `collapse_reach_km` (the collapse's transport, PLT-254 and PLT-257), whether or not an orogeny holds it up; only what finds no room founders. Ocean floor filled to the normal column turns continental, so a belt that a long collision thickens past the limit widens its continent instead of losing the crust. Since 2026-10-18 a receiver fills only up to its step ceiling (PLT-287), and the founder's and the rate limit's losses share `collapse::sink`. Since 2026-10-20 the receiver's own ocean crust stays under it as its basement (PLT-331), and a sink takes the basement with the mafic rock it takes. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/collapse.rs: founder, spread | rule; test the_thickest_crust_spreads_before_it_founders | rule | Sources of PLT-257 and PLT-258: no crust is much thicker than Tibet's (Mooney, Prodehl & Pavlenkova 2002), and thickened orogens spread outwards by lower-crustal flow over about 150 km in 15 Myr (Rey, Teyssier & Whitney 2010); a growing plateau widens while convergence goes on. Which of spreading and foundering comes first is the model's. The excess fills continental crust up to `collapse_thickness_km` (PLT-256: crust thicker than that does not stay, so the belt sheds its lower crust without building a second plateau around it) and ocean floor up to the normal column. Filling to the limit itself (70 km) was measured and dropped (2026-10-05): it widened every collision belt into a 70 km plateau, 0.35 of the land above 2 km at 500 Myr. Today's losses of continental crust at all collision zones are about 0.7 km3/yr, of which about 0.3 km3/yr is deep continental crust (Stern & Scholl 2010, full text, Figure 3 and p. 19, after Scholl & von Huene 2009): the limit should remove little. Before this rule the model foundered about 14 km3/yr (Earth-normalized, 500 Myr); with it about 6 km3/yr, still many times Earth's figure (build log 2026-10-05). | code comment; build log 2026-10-05 (plate task 7) | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-05) |

### the eclogite root and whole-column isostasy (added 2026-10-06, plate task 7; Ben: "Fix isostasy first, then land")

| PLT-278 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The pressure past which the mafic lower crust of a continental column turns (in part, PLT-280) to eclogite; the depth is this pressure over the crust's mean density (PLT-281) and gravity, 54 km at Earth's. Note (the M5 lane, 2026-10-25; the cold line is parked, a note only, no fetch): on Earth's cold platforms the crust past about 40 km carries almost no height (CRUST1.0: 1 to 27 m/km from 35-40 to 45-55 km against Airy's 142, PLT-281), as if it were rock of about 3,200 to 3,300 kg/m3, a dense crustal base this pressure-only, one-share eclogite rule does not make in cold cratons. Pointers, not read in full: Durrheim & Mooney 1994, Stadtlander et al. 1999, Hacker et al. 2015. The datum and ISO-110's datum-frame heights are in ISO-110's note. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: ECLOGITE_PA, eclogite_m | 1.5e9 | Pa | Wittlinger et al. 2009 (Geophys. J. Int. 177:1037, full text): "If the required temperatures (~600° to ~700°) and pressures (~15 to ~20 kbars) are plausible in the lower crust at ~60 km depth beneath southern Tibet (Mechie et al. 2004)", the lower end taken; Shi F. et al. 2018 (Nat. Commun. 9:3483, full text): "below ~60 km the lower crust may have reached roughly 30% eclogitization". Neither states a gabbro to eclogite boundary as a measured number: the sources stress that kinetics and fluids, not the pressure alone, decide (Krystopowicz & Currie 2013, full text; Leech 2001, snippet only). Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-05. | code comment; build log 2026-10-06 (plate task 7, the eclogite root) | `cited-unverified` |
| PLT-279 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The density of eclogitized mafic crust, for the pull of the root: an eclogite thickness E lowers the surface by E (rho_e - rho_c) / rho_m below its Airy height with the mean crustal density. Note (the M5 lane, 2026-10-25; the cold line is parked, a note only, no fetch): on Earth's cold platforms the crust past about 40 km carries almost no height (CRUST1.0: 1 to 27 m/km from 35-40 to 45-55 km against Airy's 142, PLT-281), as if it were rock of about 3,200 to 3,300 kg/m3, a dense crustal base this pressure-only, one-share eclogite rule does not make in cold cratons. Pointers, not read in full: Durrheim & Mooney 1994, Stadtlander et al. 1999, Hacker et al. 2015. The datum and ISO-110's datum-frame heights are in ISO-110's note. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: RHO_ECLOGITE, root_drop_m | 3450 | kg/m3 | Shi D. et al. 2020 (PNAS 117:24742, full text): "mafic eclogite (3,450 kg/m3) (values taken from ref. 16)", ref. 16 being Hetényi et al. 2007; Krystopowicz & Currie 2013 (EPSL 361:195, full text), Table 1: eclogite 3450 (their text: 3500-3600 for mafic eclogite); Buntin et al. 2021 (Nat. Commun., full text): "common values of 3.45-3.50 g/cm3 reported worldwide". Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-05. | code comment; build log 2026-10-06 | `cited-unverified` |
| PLT-280 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The share of the mafic crust deeper than the eclogite pressure (PLT-278) that is eclogite. One share for every column, at once: the model has no temperature, fluid or time dependence of the reaction. Note (the M5 lane, 2026-10-25; the cold line is parked, a note only, no fetch): on Earth's cold platforms the crust past about 40 km carries almost no height (CRUST1.0: 1 to 27 m/km from 35-40 to 45-55 km against Airy's 142, PLT-281), as if it were rock of about 3,200 to 3,300 kg/m3, a dense crustal base this pressure-only, one-share eclogite rule does not make in cold cratons. Pointers, not read in full: Durrheim & Mooney 1994, Stadtlander et al. 1999, Hacker et al. 2015. The datum and ISO-110's datum-frame heights are in ISO-110's note. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: ECLOGITE_SHARE, eclogite_m | 0.3 | share | Shi D. et al. 2020 (full text): the Indian lower crust under southern Tibet is "only ~30% eclogitized so gravitationally stable"; Shi F. et al. 2018 (full text): "roughly 30% eclogitization, assuming a homogeneous mixture", and "Actual degrees of eclogitization depend on temperature, age, and volatiles and therefore vary". Conflicting: Wittlinger et al. 2009 (abstract) read the same layer as fully eclogitic; Krystopowicz & Currie 2013 (full text) give 20-90 % for observed density contrasts, "eclogitization occurs heterogeneously". The best-supported single value (two full texts on the one measured case) was taken. Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-05. | code comment; build log 2026-10-06 | `cited-unverified` |
| PLT-281 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The continental crust's mean density for whole-column isostasy: when crust is stacked onto a column, spread from it or founders (belts, collapse, the thickest-crust limit), its height changes by the thickness change over rho_m / (rho_m - rho_c_mean) = 7.0 (142 m of height per km of crust), with the mantle at 3,300 (ISO-043). The cordillera roots under arcs use it too (PLT-051). Erosion from the top of a column keeps the upper crust's 2,700 (ISO-008, 5.5). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: RHO_CRUST_MEAN, COLUMN_ISOSTATIC_FACTOR, isostatic_change_m; plates/belts.rs: stack_front; plates/collapse.rs: founder, spread, iso_height | 2830 (factor 3300 / 470 = 7.02) | kg/m3 | Christensen & Mooney 1995 (J. Geophys. Res. 100:9761, abstract, library ChristensenMooney1995): "The mean crustal density calculated from our model is 2830 kg/m3"; checked against Hyndman & Currie 2011 (Geology 39:783, full text): elevation against crustal thickness "131±12 m/km for the Cordillera and 141±27 m/km for stable areas"; Luffi & Ducea 2022 (Rev. Geophys., full text, arcs): the Airy slope "requires rho_m/rho_c = 1.15, which is satisfied by a 2.83-2.87 g/cm3 arc crust underlain by a 3.25-3.30 g/cm3 lithospheric mantle". Read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-05. | code comment; build log 2026-10-06 | `cited-unverified` |
| PLT-282 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The eclogite root: the column's mafic pools (mafic plutonic and mafic volcanic, which the model keeps at the bottom of the column) lying deeper than the eclogite depth (PLT-278) are eclogite at the share PLT-280; the root lowers the surface by the root's thickness x (rho_e - rho_c_mean) / rho_m (PLT-279, PLT-281). Applied wherever a column's thickness changes as a whole (belts, collapse, foundering), as a change of the root's pull between before and after. The root stays crust (it is not removed: at 30 % it is lighter than the mantle, Shi D. et al. 2020). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: eclogite_m, root_drop_m, isostatic_change_m; test a_thickened_column_grows_an_eclogite_root_below_its_pressure | E = 0.3 x min(mafic, T - 1.5e9 / (2830 g)); drop = E x 620 / 3300 | rule | Sources of PLT-278 to PLT-281. Which pools stand for the lower crust, and the one share for every column, are the model's. A 50 km column has no root at Earth's gravity (the threshold is 54 km), so the root does not bring a 50 km crust to the Urals' height: the sources put the Urals' low stand (crust 50-58 km, Carbonell et al. 1996 and 2000, abstracts; mean altitude 0.5-0.8 km, Artyushkov et al. 2000, snippet only) down to dense mafic rock at the base of the crust that is not eclogite (Stadtlander, Mechie & Schulze 1999, abstract; "the base of the Uralian crust has not undergone major eclogitization", Leech 2001, snippet only) and to the cold lithosphere of old orogens (Hyndman & Currie 2011, full text: the thermal state moves elevation by about 1,600 m at equal thickness). | code comment; build log 2026-10-06 | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-06; the rule's physics is sourced, its mapping onto the pools is the model's) |
| PLT-283 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The land's gap budget (measurement only, on with the environment variable DEEPTIME_GAP_BUDGET): each sample's gap, its elevation less the Airy height of its own column (`collapse::iso_height`, PLT-254 and PLT-281/282), is kept split into parts by the process that changed it. Every process of a plate step is bracketed and books its change of gap; the surface stage's feedback is split into the rock eroded or deposited, its local isostatic rebound and the local response to the rest of the change of sea water and ice (`flexure::local_answers`, the Airy answers to the same loads; the water the roots' crust pushed aside apart), the regional part of the flexure's answer to those loads, the flexure's bending of the roots the plate model and the spreading put in the elevation (with the sea water their crust pushed aside, so that locally, on land and under the sea, they answer nothing), its answer to the moment a weakened plate releases (the step's loads, the roots and the released moment each solved apart, `flexure::StepIn::parts`, at a tenth of the step's tolerance), the rest of the flexure (the loads other stages laid since the last step, among them the volcano stage's cones and the impacts' craters, and the solves' unfinished parts, carried in the unsolved residual), the plateau spreading, what the stages after the surface stage add to the surface and hand to the plate model with it (the volcano stage's cones and lava, the impacts' craters and ejecta: `surface/gap_later_m`) and a carry (advection on L1, sampling, clamps); a resampling blends the parts with the column's own weights and books the gap its blending adds and the gap of the crust it accretes. The rate limit (PLT-287, PLT-288) is its own part, `rate` (2026-10-18). Each part also keeps what of it was made while the sample was oceanic crust. Reported per thickness band every 50 Myr: the stock (the mean parts of the continental samples and their ocean shares) and the rate (m/Myr per column, of the gap and of the elevation). The spike record (2026-10-18): every step's largest rise and thickening of a visible column, overall and per part, the steps whose largest rise passed 5, 10, 20 and 30 km, and a line for every step past 5 km of rise or 35 km of thickening (`gap_spikes`, `gap_spike` lines). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/gap_budget.rs; plates/mod.rs (`GAP_SNAPSHOT_MYR`, the bookings in `advance`, `apply_surface_feedback`); plates/ridges.rs (the resampling's traces); surface/mod.rs and surface/flexure.rs (the surface's parts as fields, `local_answers`, the split solves); surface/volcanoes/mod.rs and surface/impacts/mod.rs (their change of the surface into `surface/gap_later_m`); crates/deeptime-cli/src/run.rs (`gap_budget`, `gap_stock`, `gap_rate` lines in the run summary); tools/review/gap-budget.py (the tables) | bands 0-30-40-55-70-inf km; every 50 Myr; spike bands 5, 10, 20, 30 km (`SPIKE_EDGES_M`) | m, m/Myr | none (diagnostic split) | code comment | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-17: report classes for reading which process holds the land's gap; nothing in the simulation reads them, and with the variable unset the run is unchanged, test `the_gap_budget_changes_nothing_and_adds_up`) |
| PLT-284 | crust and isostasy (plates) | A resampling's accretion stands up: where a visible continental column receives crust at a resampling (hidden continental crust or sediment under it, or visible crust of the same or a subducting plate; `pools::accrete`), its surface rises by the Airy change of the crust added (whole-column factor, PLT-281, and the eclogite root, PLT-282), towards and never past the column's Airy height (`collapse::iso_height`) and not above `max_elevation_m`, and the rise is handed to the surface as uplift (`uplift_mm_yr`), as the collapse raises its receivers (PLT-256). Crust a collision already stacked (`stacked`) is consumed and raises nothing. Since 2026-10-18 the rate limit runs after the accretion (PLT-287, PLT-288): a column past its blended step ceiling spreads or loses the excess, and none rises more than 3.5 mm/yr over the step; before it the accretion gave a column up to 566 km of crust and 36 km of rise in one step, and the run's forced last resampling left its peak at 27 to 29 km (build log 2026-10-18 "Plate spikes"). ### the fastest a column thickens and rises (added 2026-10-18, the plate spikes)

| PLT-287 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The fastest a column's crust thickens in a plate step. When a step begins every sample's ceiling is its thickness plus this rate times the step. After the step's thickening processes (belts, arcs, the founder and the collapse, the transition, the collisions, the hotspots) and again after a resampling's accretion (the ceilings blended with the column's own resampling weights), a visible column above its ceiling moves the excess to the nearest room on its plate within `collapse_reach_km` (`collapse::spread`, as PLT-277: continental crust up to `crust_max_km`, ocean floor up to the normal column, each under its own ceiling), and what finds no room goes from the bottom of the column to the mantle (`collapse::sink`, as PLT-258). A column that releases crust sinks by the Airy change of what it lost, never below where it stood when the step began (after a resampling, the blended surface before the accretion). The collapse's and the founder's receivers fill only up to their ceilings. Before 2026-10-18 nothing bounded it: a resampling's accretion gave one column up to 566 km of crust in a 2 Myr step, the collision wedge 35 to 94 km. Counted in `plates/rate_capped_km3` (the excess) and `plates/rate_lost_km3` (the part that went to the mantle). | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.thickening_max_mm_yr; plates/rate.rs: begin, limit, end; plates/collapse.rs: spread, sink, held; plates/ridges.rs: resample (the limits carried across); plates/mod.rs: advance; plates/gap_budget.rs: Part::Rate; test no_column_ends_a_step_thicker_than_its_ceiling | 5.0 (default and every built-in preset; 0 = no limit); 10 km in a 2 Myr step | mm/yr (km/Myr) | Garzione, Molnar, Libarkin & MacFadden 2006 (EPSL 241:543, full text, read 2026-10-18), p. 551, eq. 2: "u H = W dH / dt"; with "u = 10 mm/yr [21] over a plateau width of W = 300 km and an initial crustal thickness of H = 50 km" the Altiplano's crust thickens at about 1.7 mm/yr, and "crustal thickening of 8 km over ~4 Myr could raise the surface ~1.2 km". Sundell et al. 2021 (GSA Today 31(6), full text through the web fetch tool, 2026-10-18): southern Tibet's "crustal thickness increased by ~40 km at ~1.3 mm/a between 60 and 30 Ma"; its kernel model "increases from ~40 to ~70 km between 60 and 40 Ma" (1.5 mm/yr). The thickest crust on Earth was built at 1 to 2 mm/yr over its whole width. 5 mm/yr is about three times that, for one lattice column over one step rather than a plateau over millions of years; where the excess goes is the model's. | code comment (plates/rate.rs); build log 2026-10-18 "Plate spikes" | `game-choice` (agent, plate spikes, 2026-10-18: a margin over the two regional rates, which were read; measured 2026-10-18 (plate spikes, `earthlike_quick_16k`, 500 Myr, six runs): no column thickened more than 10.0 km in a step (master: up to 566 km); 11.6 km3 a year passed the ceilings, of which 4.3 went to the mantle, and the founder's loss fell from 4.1 to 1.8 km3 a year; continental crust 0.393 of the planet at the end against master's 0.406; over 1000 Myr 16.4 km3 a year passed the ceilings, 6.3 went to the mantle, the founder's loss fell from 6.6 to 2.3, and continental crust ended at 0.405 against 0.433: the loss to the mantle in all rose from 4.1 to 6.1 (500 Myr) and from 6.6 to 8.5 km3 a year (1000 Myr), a follow-up; measured again 2026-10-18 after the resampling lumps (PLT-289 to PLT-292; six runs, master and fix both with the polar holes' fix): 6.0 km3 a year passed the ceilings at 500 Myr and 0.53 went to the mantle (master 11.9 and 4.07), 9.2 and 0.75 at 1000 Myr (master 16.5 and 6.05); the founder and the rate limit together 2.1 and 3.0 km3 a year (master 5.5 and 8.3), against about 0.7 at Earth's collision zones, PLT-258) |
| PLT-288 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The fastest a column's surface rises in a plate step: no visible column ends the step's processes, or a resampling's accretion, higher than it stood when the step began plus this rate times the step. A process that lifts a column faster (a belt filling a foreland, an accretion bringing a column to its Airy height at once) leaves it there, below its Airy height, and later steps let it rise the rest. After a resampling the top is blended with the column's resampling weights and the cut is taken out of the step's `uplift_mm_yr`. The surface stage's own changes (erosion, rebound, flexure) are not limited, nor is any fall. Counted in `plates/rate_held_km3` (km2 × m / 1000 held down). | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.uplift_max_mm_yr; plates/rate.rs: limit (top_m); plates/ridges.rs: resample; plates/mod.rs: advance; test no_surface_rises_above_its_top | 3.5 (default and every built-in preset; 0 = no limit); 7 km in a 2 Myr step | mm/yr (km/Myr) | Garzione et al. 2006 (full text, read 2026-10-18), p. 551: "A 2.5 to 3.5 km rise of the Altiplano in a period as short 1 to 4 Myr implies an average vertical speed between 3.5 and 0.6 mm/yr"; abstract, p. 543: "Only the removal of dense eclogitic lower crust and mantle lithosphere can generate a change of this magnitude and rapidity"; crustal thickening alone gives about 0.3 mm/yr there (p. 551, eq. 2). The cap is the fastest of these rates, which on Earth needed the loss of the root. | code comment (plates/rate.rs); build log 2026-10-18 "Plate spikes" | `verified` (Garzione et al. 2006 p. 551, the value read 2026-10-18; its use as one cap for every column is `game-choice`, agent, plate spikes; measured 2026-10-18 (plate spikes, 500 Myr, six runs): the highest cell at the end 7,288 m on L1 (master 23,544), 6,071 m after the young stage (master 8,203); the largest rise in a step 7,000 m (master up to 35,842); over 1000 Myr the highest cell at the end 8,089 m on L1 (master 25,248), at most 13,752 m) |

### resampling lumps (added 2026-10-18, the follow-up to the plate spikes)

| PLT-289 | subduction | Ocean floor as thick as a continent turns continental at a contact: each step, before the overlaps are resolved, a sample at a plate contact (either side of an overlap) that is not continental and holds at least a normal continental column's crystalline crust (T_n, 33 km, the transition's PLT-261) becomes continental, whatever stacked it there (a resampling's accretion, a slab's accreted crust, an arc). It then collides, takes a belt or moves as a terrane like any continental crust, instead of going under as ocean floor and coming back as one lump on the column above it at the next resampling. Counted in the run summary as `buoyant`. The threshold is T_n and not Cloos's 15 km so that the transition's ocean side (up to about 20 km of thinned margin, ocean floor by design, PLT-261) keeps its type: at 15 km two traced runs turned about ten times as many samples, the taper formed again behind each converted ring, and the continents grew (build log 2026-10-18 "Resampling lumps"). Since 2026-10-20 the sample's own ocean crust stays under it as its basement (PLT-331). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/subduction.rs: resolve; plates/transition.rs: continental_m; plates/state.rs: Counters.buoyant; plates/stats.rs: Summary; test ocean_floor_as_thick_as_a_continent_turns_continental_at_a_contact | crystalline_m(pools) >= T_n (33.0 km) at an overlap | m | Cloos 1993 (abstract) as context: "only bodies of continental and oceanic island arc crust that are > ~15 km thick make the lithosphere buoyant enough to jam a subduction zone"; "Lithosphere that is 100 km thick with a 30-km-thick granitic continental crust resists Subduction because it is ~0.09 gm/cm3 less dense than the asthenosphere". Crust of 33 km is past both; the choice of T_n is the model's | code comment; build log 2026-10-18 "Resampling lumps" | `game-choice` (agent, resampling lumps, 2026-10-18; Cloos 1993 `abstract-only`; measured 2026-10-18 (`earthlike_quick_16k`, six runs, with PLT-290 to PLT-292): 4,511 [3,483, 5,466] samples a run turned by 500 Myr and 17,092 [12,675, 21,837] by 1000 Myr; continental crust at the end 0.396 and 0.431 of the planet against master's 0.405 and 0.420) |
| PLT-290 | collisions | Only continental crust takes a collision belt's stack: the belt's receivers are the overriding plate's continental samples within the belt's width behind the front (PLT-243's profile and area floor unchanged, so with fewer receivers more of the stack goes down with the slab). Until 2026-10-18 ocean floor behind the front (beside a narrow overriding continent, or an island arc) took its share too; the dampening pulled its surface back down to the ocean floor, so it kept its room and took more every step, up to 60 km of metamorphic rock under the oceanic label, which later subducted as ocean floor and came back as one lump at a resampling. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/belts.rs: stack_front; test the_belt_stacks_only_on_continental_crust | rule | rule | none (an orogen is thickened continental crust; bookkeeping) | code comment; build log 2026-10-18 "Resampling lumps" | `game-choice` (agent, resampling lumps, 2026-10-18; measured 2026-10-18 (`earthlike_quick_16k`, six runs, with PLT-289, PLT-291 and PLT-292): the belts' crust that went down with the slab rose from 1.00 to 1.69 km3 a year at 500 Myr and from 1.30 to 2.07 at 1000 Myr) |
| PLT-291 | collisions | Crust a collision took across its front stays under: `Sample::stacked` (PLT-276) is no longer cleared each step but kept while the sample stays hidden, so a collision that ends (a parting, a terrane transfer, a weld) does not make the crossed crust into crust to accrete again. A transfer moves only the terrane's samples that have not gone under, and a weld unhides only those; the crossed crust stays hidden in its plate and the next resampling consumes it. Counted with PLT-276's in `plates/resample_stacked_km3`. Until 2026-10-18 it accreted, in lumps of a whole continental column. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/ridges.rs: resample (colliding, tally[1]); test a_resampling_consumes_the_visible_crust_a_collision_took | rule | rule | none (bookkeeping: each volume counted once) | code comment; build log 2026-10-18 "Resampling lumps" | `game-choice` (agent, resampling lumps, 2026-10-18; measured 2026-10-18 (`earthlike_quick_16k`, six runs, with PLT-289 to PLT-291): a resampling consumed 15.6 km3 a year as a collision's at 500 Myr and 21.2 at 1000 Myr (master 11.5 and 13.8), against 16.6 and 21.9 that crossed a front (master 15.5 and 18.9), and accreted 13.8 and 19.0 (master 22.1 and 28.8)) |

They are local rock (an island arc, crust accreted at a resampling, the base of a thick sediment pile, and, unless PLT-294's switch is on, the continental crust the transition parks on its ocean side), and a blend spread each a little further at every resampling, so that over a run they reached most of the sea floor. Blending every pool across kinds also put a share of a continent's granite, gneiss and platform cover into each ocean sample beside it. | PLT-294 | crust and isostasy (plates) | A switch for what the transition's ocean side is made of, on in every shipped preset since 2026-10-20 (`earthlike_*` and `pipeline_*`; see decisions log 2026-10-20) and off in the code's default, which the plate pipeline's `plates_*` presets carry (they run no structure stage, so draw no column), and in the test presets. Off: the sinks take the sources' rock (PLT-261), so the continent's granite and gneiss are parked on the oceanic samples within W/2 of a margin, between the ocean's lavas and its gabbro in the column (on the quick planet that is nearly all of its ocean), and stay in the continental crust's budget (with the switch on, the crust a resampling accreted to continents fell from 1.59 to 1.02 x 10^9 km3 over the run on seed 1, so the parked rock seems to come back that way; not traced further). On: an oceanic sink takes the same volume as ocean crust, mafic volcanic and mafic plutonic rock in the ocean column's proportion (2,000 : 5,000 m, PLT-104), the thick igneous crust of a volcanic margin, the commoner kind (Franke 2013), which the column draws as lavas over sheeted dikes over gabbro; a continental sink still takes the sources' rock. The volume moved, the taper and the heights are PLT-261 to PLT-264's either way. On, the continental rock the sources give up is not kept anywhere: on Earth it stays in the stretched continent, which the model's fixed boundary cannot widen (a wider continent would refill its own taper, the ratchet PLT-289 records at 15 km), and the land falls. Magma-poor margins (hyperextended continental crust, then exhumed mantle) are not modelled either way. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.transition_ocean_crust; crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/transition.rs: step (the sinks); test the_ocean_side_thickens_with_ocean_crust_not_the_continents_rock | true in the shipped presets, false in `TectonicParams::default`; on: MV : MP = 2 : 5 | switch; ratio | Franke 2013 (accepted manuscript, full text, read 2026-10-04): volcanic rifted margins are "the majority of passive continental margins worldwide". The volcanic-margin reading is from memory. Measured 2026-10-19 (`earthlike_quick_16k`, 1000 Myr, final checkpoints read with `deeptime dump-fields`, supercontinent seed 1): with the transition switched off (`transition_width_km` = 0) the ocean-basin L1 cells holding over 50 m of felsic plutonic rock fell from 0.83 to 0.35, so the parked rock is the main source of granite under the sea; with the switch on they are 0.45 against master's 0.80, and 0.07 of the cells on crust younger than 20 Myr against 0.42. Land at the end of the surface stage, three seeds each: supercontinent 0.140, 0.131, 0.139 on against master's 0.165, 0.189, 0.177, continental crust 7.6, 7.2, 9.0 against 8.3, 8.5, 9.3 x 10^9 km3; scattered continents 0.148 against 0.154 on seed 1. Again on master 49f5f543 (with the continents fix bc2e5472), 2026-10-20, land at the end of the run, seeds 1 to 3: supercontinent 0.277, 0.286, 0.252 on against 0.309, 0.307, 0.344 off and master's 0.264, 0.326, 0.321; scattered continents 0.221, 0.289, 0.293 on against 0.356, 0.323, 0.294 off and master's 0.289, 0.342, 0.282; means 0.27 on (both starts), 0.32 off, 0.30 on master, Earth 0.29; continental crust 7.0 to 8.3 x 10^9 km3 on against 7.8 to 9.1 off) |

### mountains eat the world (added 2026-10-20, the continent budget)

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PLT-300 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Lower-crustal flow: each plate step, on the visible continental samples of one plate (none at a trench), crust thicker than T_c spreads into the crust around it by diffusion of thickness, ∂T/∂t = ∇·(K∇T) with K = K₀ (h/h₀)³ and h = min(max(T − T_c, 0), 2 h₀) (the thin-channel flux, the channel's thickness cubed, in the isostatic limit), along each lattice edge at the diffusivity of the thicker column, in as many explicit substeps as keep it stable (a guard of 4096; until 2026-10-20 at most 64, which no 2-Myr step of the measured runs reached: 30 substeps a step on average at full 16k, 12 at quick 16k, land-shape task) and in pieces of at most 2 Myr, each finding its channels and their two rings again; the source's own rock mix moves; heights follow by Airy (a source sinks by its isostatic change but not below the Airy height of the crust it keeps, a receiver rises but not above its new Airy height). T_c = `collapse_thickness_km` (PLT-256). Volume is kept to 1e-6 (test `a_thick_belt_spreads_into_the_crust_around_it_and_keeps_its_volume`). | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.crust_channel_km2_myr; plates/flow.rs: step | K₀ = 1500 (default and the earthlike, pipeline and plates presets; 0 on the 4k test planet `planet_world_4k`, as PLT-255) | km²/Myr | Rey, Teyssier & Whitney 2010 (Lithosphere 2:328, full text read 2026-10-03, PLT-257): "Over 15 m.y., the length scale of channel-flow extrusion is 150 km rather than 1500 km"; K₀ = L²/t = 150² / 15. Inside the range of the surface stage's own spreading (ISO-022, Clark & Royden 2000: 10² to 10⁵ km²/Myr). Bird 1991 (abstract): the Basin and Range "cannot maintain short-wavelength (100 km) Moho relief of more than 1 km for times greater than 10-20 m.y. at most". Royden et al. 1997 and Clark & Royden 2000 for the channel flow out of Tibet (not fetched). | code comment; build log 2026-10-20 "Mountains eat the world" | `cited-unverified` (the quote was read, the step from a length and a time to a diffusivity is the project's) |

| PLT-310 | plate-check targets | The plate boundary's normal at a boundary sample, towards a neighbouring plate: over the sample and the visible samples of its own plate within two lattice steps, the sum of each one's lattice edges into that plate, each edge's direction times its Voronoi edge's length (PLT-222's cotangent formula), on the sample's tangent plane, normalised. A Voronoi edge is square to its lattice edge, so along a chain of boundary samples the sum is the chord of that stretch of the boundary (about five spacings) turned a right angle: a straight boundary gives its normal however the lattice zigzags, where one sample's own edges lie about 30 degrees either side of it. Read by the boundary classes (PLT-221, PLT-311) and the arcs' convergence (PLT-312). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/shortening.rs: boundary_normal, crossing_sum; subduction.rs: fronts_begin (normals); dynamics.rs: boundary_mix | n = normalize(tangent(sum over w within 2 steps on the same plate of sum over edges w-u into the other plate of l_wu d_wu)) | unit vector | none cited: geometry, as PLT-222 | code comment; build log 2026-10-20 "Land shape" | `game-choice` (agent, 2026-10-20: two rings is a numerical choice; test `the_boundary_normal_sees_a_transform`: along the boundary x = 0 of the 16k test lattice the normal is under 20 degrees off everywhere, pure strike-slip reads as a transform at more than 97 % of the boundary samples and head-on motion nowhere) |
| PLT-311 | plate-check targets | A boundary whose relative motion lies within 20 degrees of its own direction is a transform: the relative velocity's component along the boundary's normal (PLT-310) under sin 20 degrees of its speed. Such a boundary neither converges nor parts in the boundary classes (PLT-221), on an active contact too. Bird applies the 20 degrees to the boundaries that are not subduction zones and calls a step a subduction zone where it converges at all and has a Benioff zone or a Quaternary arc, which he marked by hand; the model's contact belongs to a pair of plates, not to a stretch of their boundary, so the 20 degrees stand in for the missing Benioff zone along a contact's strike-slip stretches (a subduction zone more oblique than 70 degrees from its normal counts as transform here). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/shortening.rs: is_transform; dynamics.rs: boundary_mix | 20 degrees | degrees | Bird 2003 (G3 4(3):1027), paragraph [127]: "Boundary steps are considered strike-slip if the computed relative velocity (based on the Euler poles of Table 1) has an azimuth within ±20 of the azimuth of the plate boundary step. The slab's volume entering a trench per km of it is its thickness times this normal component, so a front on an oblique or strike-slip stretch feeds its arc in proportion. Measured (land-shape task, 2026-10-20, quick 16k, seeds 1 to 3, both starts): the arcs' g-weighted front is 0.54 of the full-speed one; island arcs 2.5 km³ a year against 4.0 (Earth 1.5) and continental arcs 0.9 against 1.5 (Earth 1.0), but the land falls from 0.31 to 0.24 of the planet, the continental crust from 0.42 to 0.30, and the land above 2 km rises from 0.32 to 0.42; so it waits for the continental crust's losses to be brought to Earth's. Land and high ground move away from Earth, so it stays off (decisions log 2026-10-20). Measured again by the arc-basement task (2026-10-20, the same ensemble on the branch with PLT-330 and PLT-331 and the presets' PLT-294 on; off against on, supercontinent / scattered): island arcs 3.24 to 2.81 km³ a year, continental arcs 1.62 to 1.15; land 0.234 / 0.242 to 0.210 / 0.203, land above 2 km 0.312 / 0.324 to 0.335 / 0.362, coast p50 159 / 159 to 165 / 160 km, plate checks 28 to 27 of 45 (with PLT-294 off: land 0.278 / 0.251 to 0.228 / 0.249, above 2 km 0.414 / 0.374 to 0.445 / 0.386, coast 165 / 150 to 185 / 159 km, plate checks 29 to 30). | PLT-331 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Continental crust made from ocean floor keeps the ocean's own crust under it as its basement: a sample that turns continental from ocean floor (an island arc matured, PLT-047; ocean floor as thick as a continent at a contact, PLT-289; ocean floor filled to the normal column by the collapse's spread, PLT-277) records min(the ocean column's 7 km, its mafic rock) as `basement_m`. The basement stays in the column's mafic pools, so the column's height (isostasy and `settle.rs`, ISO-078), its rock and its kind are as before, but it is no continental crust: the crust budget leaves it out of the continental volume (PLT-302), and a collision stacks only the crust on top (the belts' thickness and pools less the basement, PLT-247 and PLT-248), so the basement goes down with the slab. It thins with its column (the transition's and the collapse's sources, PLT-289's sources), is never more than the column's mafic rock (what eroded, foundered or flowed took it), blends between same-kind corners at a resampling, and is 0 on ocean floor and on the continents' own crust. A matured arc still turns continental, emerges and accretes to a continent at a collision. Heights stay on the whole column on purpose: 7 km of ocean crust settles at −5.07 km under water (old ocean floor), so an arc on it stands by its own crust's buoyancy; leaving the basement out of the height would drop every arc on old floor about 1.5 km (7 km / 7 x 3300 / 2270, the agent's arithmetic). | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/state.rs: Sample::basement_m, make_continental, basement, own_thickness_m, own_pools; mod.rs: finish_samples; subduction.rs: resolve; collapse.rs: spread, sink; transition.rs; ridges.rs: interpolate; belts.rs: stack_front; crust_budget.rs; tools/review/loss-budget.py | min(7,000 m, the column's mafic rock) | m | Stern & Scholl 2010 (Int. Geol. Rev. 52:1-31, read in full), p. 7, of the intra-oceanic arcs' growth rates: "The largest sources of error are as follows: (1) uncertainty about whether to include an original 6–7 km thickness of ‘trapped’ oceanic crust"; "early concepts assumed that new arcs grew on a substrate of pre-existing oceanic crust that was trapped when the new subduction zone formed (Dickinson and Sealy 1979). More recent considerations indicate that the substrate for intra-oceanic arcs is produced when subduction begins"; Figure 6 caption (after Tatsumi et al. 2008): "Incipient arc magmatism replaces the pre-existing oceanic crust to create the initial mafic arc crust". So Table 1's 1.5 km³ a year (100 km³/km/Myr over 14,500 km, PLT-045) is the arc's crust, with any ocean crust under it at most once; the model's arc rate adds that, and the ocean column under it is not counted again. The basement at a collision: Cloos 1993 (abstract) on "Oceanic island arc complexes built upon ocean crust" and the buoyant crust over ~15 km that jams a trench; ocean crust "thinner (∼7 km), denser (∼2.90 g/cc)" (Stern & Scholl, Introduction); from memory, not read: Boutelier, Chemenda & Burg 2003 (EPSL 212:31-45), an arc reaching a trench loses its lower part to the slab while its upper crust accretes | code comment; build log 2026-10-20 "The arc's basement and the arcs' fronts"; plates.md "The arc's basement and the arcs' fronts (2026-10-20)" | `from-memory` (Stern & Scholl's p. 7 and Figure 6 read 2026-10-20; the basement going down with the slab rests on Boutelier et al. 2003, not read; tests `arcs_turn_continental_by_their_crust_not_their_height` (a matured arc keeps 7 km of basement) and `a_basement_goes_down_with_the_slab`. Measured (2026-10-20, `earthlike_quick_16k`, 1000 Myr, seeds 1 to 3, both starts, the presets' PLT-294 on, master d9bd2b65 against the branch with PLT-330 and PLT-331; supercontinent / scattered), with PLT-330: the loss budget's "ocean floor made continental" row falls from 4.33 to 0.14 km³ a year (Table 1: none; the rest is ocean floor thinner than 7 km when it turned), 3.71 km³ a year stays under the arcs as basement, and the collisions lose 1.85 and 1.83 (Earth 0.7). PLT-331 alone (the branch less PLT-330's test): the row falls from 4.33 to 0.12 with 4.09 kept as basement, land 0.271 / 0.272 to 0.267 / 0.273, land above 2 km 0.348 / 0.297 to 0.315 / 0.313, coast p50 149 / 145 to 168 / 164 km, plate checks 28 to 29 of 45, the collisions' p90 121 to 115 Myr (at most 120), continental crust 0.411 / 0.405 to 0.385 / 0.392) |

### the ocean floor's age-depth law (added 2026-10-21, sea level from the ocean's volume)

| PLT-350 | crust and isostasy (plates) | Which law sets the ocean floor's height: `dampening` (Cortial's relaxation, PLT-129) or `gdh1` (Stein & Stein's age-depth law as a subsidence rate, PLT-352). Every shipped Earth-like and pipeline preset takes `gdh1` with GDH1's ridge (-2,600 m) and old floor (-5,651 m), where the floor is GDH1 exactly (other depths: PLT-355); the Rust default and the four `plates/plates_*` presets keep `dampening`, so the plate model's own reproductions and checks are unchanged. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: OceanDepth, TectonicParams.ocean_depth, validate; presets | gdh1 (shipped presets); dampening (default) | - | none needed (a switch) | code comment; build log 2026-10-21 | `game-choice` (agent, build log 2026-10-21; the brief: PLT-129's dampening fixed at an age-depth law) |
| PLT-351 | ridges and ocean crust | New ocean floor is laid at the law's depth for its age: the first ocean (age from the distance to its ridge, PLT-018), the ridges' new floor and the L1 sampling's gaps (the age the gap's floor would have) take `floor_at(age)`, -d(age) under GDH1 (PLT-352; between other depths PLT-355) or the dampening's closed form under Cortial's law. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: floor_at; plates/init.rs, plates/ridges.rs, plates/l1.rs | z = -d(age) (gdh1 at GDH1's depths); z = ocean_elevation(age, ridge, floor, damping) (dampening) | m | as PLT-352 | code comment | `cited-unverified` (as PLT-352) |
| PLT-352 | ridges and ocean crust | GDH1: bare ocean floor under water stands at d(t) = 2,600 + 365 sqrt(t) m below the sea up to 20 Myr and 5,651 - 2,473 exp(-0.0278 t) after (the branches meet within 0.3 m). The floor's subsidence is its rate: each step an oceanic sample sinks by d(t) - d(t - dt) at its age t (the step's end), whatever stands on it, so a load (sediment, a seamount, thickened crust) rides down with the floor instead of being relaxed away (PLT-129). Pelagic sediment and the trench's pull are unchanged. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: gdh1_depth_m, GDH1_RIDGE_M, GDH1_FLOOR_M, GDH1_KNEE_MYR, surface_step | d = 2600 + 365 t^0.5 (t < 20); 5651 - 2473 e^(-0.0278 t) (t >= 20); dz = -(d(t) - d(t - dt)) | m; Myr | Stein & Stein 1992, Nature 359:123-129 | code comment | `cited-unverified` (the paper is paywalled and not read; read by a research sub-agent, 2026-10-21: Stein 1995, "Heat Flow of the Earth", AGU Reference Shelf 1, Table 2: ridge depth 2600 m, and the heat flow law's decay 48 + 96 exp(-0.0278 t); 365, 5,651, 2,473 and the 20-Myr knee only on a secondary page that cites the paper; Parsons & Sclater 1977's abstract, read, gives the older 2500 + 350 t^0.5 and 6400 - 3200 exp(-t/62.8)) |
| PLT-353 | ridges and ocean crust | Ocean floor standing above the sea (a plateau, an arc, a filled basin) sinks by the floor's water-loaded subsidence divided by W = rho_m/(rho_m - rho_w): GDH1's depths are water-loaded, and the same thermal contraction under air sinks the surface less by that factor. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: surface_step (`super::settle::WATER_LOADED`) | dz_dry = dz / W, W = 3300/(3300 - 1030) = 1.454 (settle.rs's densities) | dimensionless | Kominz et al. 1998, Geology (the backstripping relation R2 = (R1 - TS)(rho_a - rho_w)/rho_a); the inverse 1.446 in the Kansas Geological Survey's backstripping (Greenlee) | code comment | `verified` (the form, read by a research sub-agent 2026-10-21 in Kominz et al. 1998; the densities are ISO-043's and ISO-044's) |
| PLT-354 | ridges and ocean crust | The floor in a gap between plates (cells no plate covers between resamplings, `plates/gap`) belongs to no plate until the next resampling turns it into samples: the surface holds a gap cell in place instead of carrying it with the nearest plate, its ground is the L1 sampling's gap floor (deepening with its age, PLT-351) plus what the surface laid on it, and a cell that enters a gap starts bare. The L1 sampling finds a gap cell's far side at any distance (the nearest sample of each other plate), as the resampling's graph search does, instead of within its 32 nearest samples. | crates/deeptime-core/src/surface/advect.rs: hold_gaps, newborn, carried_elevation; surface/mod.rs (`surface/crust_gap`); climate/mod.rs; plates/l1.rs (GAP_FIELD, the per-plate trees) | - | - | none needed (the plate model's own kinematics: the gap's floor is the floor the next resampling makes) | code comment; build log 2026-10-21 | `game-choice` (agent, build log 2026-10-21; measured 2026-10-21: carried with the nearest plate and its zero uplift, the gap kept the edge it was pulled from and never sank under GDH1's rate form, 200 to 450 m too high; within 32 nearest samples a wide gap's cells took the near edge's depth and the gap's whole age, and the sea jumped 40 to 50 m at each resampling) |
| PLT-355 | ridges and ocean crust | GDH1's shape between the preset's ridge crest and old floor: under `gdh1` the floor is drawn from `ridge_elevation_m` (z_r) and flattens towards `oceanic_floor_elevation_m` (z_f), z(t) = z_r - s (d(t) - 2,600) and each step's sink s (d(t) - d(t - dt)), s = (z_r - z_f)/(5,651 - 2,600), so the editor's ridge and abyssal depths move the curve's ends and keep GDH1's ages; s = 1 at GDH1's own depths (every shipped preset), where the floor is GDH1 exactly. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/pools.rs: gdh1_scale, floor_at, surface_step | s = 1 (every shipped preset: -2,600 and -5,651) | dimensionless | none (a linear stretch of PLT-352's curve, not a physical law) | code comment; build log 2026-10-21 | `game-choice` (agent, build log 2026-10-21: the workbench editor's ridge and abyssal depth knobs were refused under `gdh1`, which required GDH1's depths) |
| PLT-356 | plate setup | The starting ocean floor's ages under `gdh1`: the earlier motion's distance law (PLT-273) gives their order (young where it diverged), and the floor takes the steady-state age–area distribution of an ocean made at a constant rate and consumed evenly at every age, dA/dt = C0 (1 - t/t_m): the sample of rank F (equal-area samples, youngest first) is t_m (1 - sqrt(1 - F)) old, a mean of t_m/3 and a median of 0.29 t_m. The dampening's presets (the plate-only ones) keep the distance law's ages. Without it the first ocean's median age was 19 to 22 Myr (earthlike_quick_16k) and the volume sea (ISO-085) started 330 to 450 m high on the quick planets and 605 m on the climate test's 3,000-sample planet, where it left no land and a 1-bar, 76 °C hothouse. | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/init.rs: steady_state_ages, START_OCEAN_MAX_AGE_MYR, initial_state | t_m = 180 (mean 60, median 52.7) | Myr | Parsons 1982, JGR 87(B1):289-302 (the law); t_m from memory (Earth's oldest ocean floor in place, about 180 Myr; the distance law's own cap) | code comment; sea-level.md "The first ocean" | `abstract-only` (Parsons 1982's abstract read on OSTI, 2026-10-21: "dA/dt = C0(1-(t/tm)), where C0 is the rate of crustal generation and tm the maximum age", consumption per unit age "approx. 1.7 x 10^-8 km2/yr2" = C0/t_m, which with Earth's 3.4 km2/yr of production (carbon.rs, Bird 2003) gives t_m about 200 Myr; check: the mean 60 Myr against Seton et al. 2020's 64.2 Myr, abstract read) |

England & Molnar 1997 (Science 278:647, abstract, OpenAlex, read 2026-10-22): "an estimate of 10 22 pascal second for the average viscosity of the Tibetan lithosphere". Sonder & Jones 1999 (Annu. Rev. Earth Planet. Houseman & England 1986 (JGR 91:3651, abstract, OpenAlex, read 2026-10-22): "For n > 3 and Ar between 1 and 10 a plateau of thickened crust is formed in front of the indenter". ### the lithosphere's thermal state (added 2026-10-23, gap 4)

| PLT-460 | lithosphere thermal state | Each sample carries the depth of its thermal lithosphere's base L and its depleted keel K. Each plate step, after the hotspots and before the settling: ocean floor takes L from its age (PLT-466) and no keel; a visible continental sample flagged ARC, SUBDUCTION_ZONE or HOTSPOT is heated towards max(L_hot, K) over τ_h (convective erosion, which a keel resists), one flagged DIVERGENT towards L_hot (a rift stretches the keel too: K follows L down); any other cools towards L_cold over τ_c. A continental sample with no state takes L_ref (ISO-111) and no keel. The start's continents begin at L_ref with K = min(K_0, L_ref). A resampling copies both from the major corner. On by default since 2026-10-23 (decisions log 2026-10-23, "Always, fix V1 elsewhere": physics on once built and correct; `--set tectonics.thermal_state=false` turns it off) | crates/deeptime-core/src/plates/thermal.rs: step, advance, start; plates/state.rs: Sample::lithosphere_km, keel_km; params.rs: TectonicParams.thermal_state; tests a_back_arc_heats_new_crust_in_tens_of_myr_and_it_cools_over_hundreds, a_keeled_craton_behind_an_arc_stays_cold_and_stiff | L(t + dt) = L* + (L(t) − L*) e^(−dt/τ) | km | McKenzie 1978 (EPSL 40:25, full text): the plate model's lithosphere and its thermal time; Jordan 1978 (Nature 274:544, abstract only): root zones "stabilised against convective disruption by the depletion of the continental upper mantle in a basalt-like component"; Lee 2011 (Annu. S is symmetric about 1/2 like the ramp, so the step's crust is held; W, τ, the catchments and the height line are PLT-261 to PLT-264's. | PLT-502 | plate boundaries | Ridge segments normal to the motion, joined by transforms (V1 gap 2's stage A): at a resampling, the gap points between two plates a < b are put in the frame of the pair's relative rotation, ê = (ω_a − ω_b) / ‖ω_a − ω_b‖ (colatitude θ, longitude φ); the gap is cut into bands of θ `ridge_segment_km` wide, offset per pair by a hash of the two ids; in each band the ridge stands at the circular mean of the gap's φ, a point on its +φ side goes to a, the rest to b, and its α (age and height, Cortial's) follows its great-circle distance from that meridian over the distance to its plate's edge. A band whose points nearest a do not lie on the +φ side of b's keeps the midline, as do pairs spreading under 1 mm/yr, and a point whose band's meridian lies beyond the other plate's edge (at a band's end, where an oblique gap is narrower than its offset across the band, the segment does not cross that part of the gap) or more than a quarter turn round the pole from it (review fix 2026-10-23: such a point went whole to one side, a row of the gap with no ridge in it). Each band's ridge is a meridian piece, the steps between bands small-circle pieces: transforms. Replaces, in the bands it draws, the nearer-plate assignment and the midline α of PLT-100 and PLT-101 (z̄ stays). | PLT-516 | collision | Transference (`tectonics.transference`, the docking lane's stage T, V1 item 1, 2026-10-23): a continent (a terrane of `collision_suture_frac` or more, PLT-078) in a collision that has not stalled moves alone to the overriding plate, as a smaller terrane does (PLT-068's transfer: its samples into a new rigid group of the overriding plate, PLT-080's momentum, the crossed crust left under, PLT-276), once (1) its subduction has failed: the contact has no slab left (`state.slabs` with no live track of the pair, so PLT-184's breakoff or none grown), marked at the first step it is seen (`Collision.jam_km`, the shortening then); (2) its plate has gone on converging on it by PLT-518's critical convergence since (the shortening since the jam); and (3) the collision's plastic resistance this step (`forces::Answer::collision_n`, the front's continental links' force before linearisation) over the length of the continent's visible margin against its own plate's visible ocean floor reaches PLT-517's force (`collision::transference_due`). The continent's ocean floor stays on its plate, and the force balance decides what the new boundary does. A continent with no ocean floor at its margin, or whose collision stalls first, welds its plate on the stall (PLT-246). Counted as `transferences` in the run summary; in the dynamics record a transference is a `collisions` entry with `frac` of `collision_suture_frac` or more that did not suture (no new field there, so the record of a run with the switch off is unchanged), and its collision or accretion event carries a `transference` parameter. | PLT-534 | rifting | A boundary belongs to the lithosphere on its two sides (`tectonics.split_inheritance`, the front lane, 2026-10-24): when a plate splits (a fission at a resampling, `ridges::split_cut_plates`, PLT-532; a rift, `rifting::rift`; a subduction initiation's new plate, `rifting::initiate`), each contact (polarity, onset, onset event, confirmation), slab (its length down the dip) and collision state the parent had with another plate goes to the pieces that border that plate: a piece borders it where at least `subduction::MIN_CONTACT` (3) of its samples, hidden ones included, have a sample of that plate within `inherit::BORDER_SPACINGS` (1.5) lattice spacings of their true positions. Every bordering pair (subducting side to subducting side) keeps the contact and the slab; the collision goes with its front, to the pair whose border lies nearest the collision's front centroid (a contact without one: nearest its overlap's centroid), the other pairs getting the contact without it (and with no Andean record or `cont_in` of their own yet). The slab is one sheet along the trench and its pull is counted per front edge (PLT-186, PLT-271), so each piece keeps the whole length down the dip and pulls at its own stretch of the trench; a copy of a slab that was at a front (no step off a front since it was last fed, `idle_myr` 0) is latent (`SlabTrack::latent`): it pulls only at its piece's own fronts and does not hang along the piece's border until its first front (a piece bordering the other plate across a ridge or a transform holds none of it), while a copy of a hanging slab hangs along its own piece's stretch, so the pieces' slab pull and suction sum to the parent's. A boundary no piece borders stays with the parent and closes as before; a pair inside one split plate (an initiation's planted slab, PLT-270) is not touched. The globals `plates/inherited_contacts_moved`, `_copied`, `plates/inherited_collisions`, `plates/inherited_slabs_moved` and `_copied` count them. | PLT-536 | ridges and ocean crust | Plate outlines finer than the lattice, carried through the resampling (`tectonics.level_set_membership`, the front lane, 2026-10-24): every sample keeps ψ, the sine of its angular distance from its group's outline (`level_set::LevelSet`, capped at `CAP_SPACINGS`, 2 lattice spacings). At a resampling a new lattice point is held, in each group's old triangle in the group's own frame, by the group whose corners' ψ weighed with the point's gnomonic barycentric weights is the largest (`level_set::margin`), not by the corner with the largest weight; the outline is the zero line of the interpolated signed distance, exact for a great circle. A new sample's ψ is its distance inside the top group's outline, or outside that of a group of another plate that would be on top of it, where nearer (a triangle with no corner of that group: the others' interpolated distance); a gap point's, its distance from the midline or the ridge segment (PLT-502); an orphan's, half a spacing. After the resampling and `ridges::bury`, `level_set::reinit` sets every ψ again from the outline: the zero crossing on each lattice edge between two groups, joined inside each triangle into great-circle arcs (three to the point where three groups weigh the same), and each sample's distance from the nearest arc of its group among its own and its neighbours' triangles. A sample whose neighbours' membership changed since the last reinitialisation (a rift, a fission, a suture, a twin group, a run's first resampling) gets `STALE_SPACINGS`, half a spacing, on every corner, which splits its triangles as the old rule does. | PLT-582 | subduction | The front on the current geometry (`tectonics.front_current_geometry`, the front lane, 2026-10-25). An overriding sample is a subduction front through a lattice neighbour of a plate with an active contact under it only while a visible sample of that plate stands within `subduction::trench_row_rad` of it now; the boundary mix drops such a neighbour by the same test when it converges, and asks nothing of an opening or a transform between the same two plates (the gap an opening leaves between resamplings is the opening's). The radius is two lattice spacings from the run's spacing: one for the step to the neighbour across the boundary, one for the trench's single hidden row (the overlaps hide the subducting plate's edge sample once it crosses the overriding outline, PLT-553). Between resamplings the lattice is frozen, so without the rule a front reads a neighbour that went under and was carried on (1,015 km away, the median, for the fronts off the current boundary on the front trace, PLT-580). The ocean-continent front then carries a band behind the margin, which each resampling drops: −29.7 % at a resampling on the trace, −3.4 % under this rule, −10.3 % at three spacings. The rule asks for the subducting plate's crust, not any other plate's as the trace did. Amended 2026-10-26 (the front lane, the coordinator's call of 2026-10-26): the force balance (`forces.rs`, `balance_step`) asks the same test of the fronts it gathers, so the slab pull, the suction, the slab resistance, the incipient and collision links, Forsyth & Uyeda's front friction (PLT-585) and the slab's growth stand on the current front, and of a hanging slab's samples and a collision's incoming side. Until then it read any lattice neighbour of a plate with an active contact, hidden or carried on, so a consumed plate kept pulling along its trench of the last resampling while its basal drag shrank with its area: on the pool's arm sp-v75 three of the four plates that bound PLT-022's guard were consumed down their own trench, pulling 5.5 to 14.3 × 10¹³ N/m of current front against the law's most of 4.5 × 10¹³ (FRC-002 × FRC-004), and the pull beyond that most was 1.7 % of all pull on base17-s4 and 2.7 % on the arm (plates.md "Speed scale 75, the calibration's first read (2026-10-26)"). With the front lane's incipient subduction (its own switch) also on, an incipient contact takes its incipient resistance alone and no PLT-585 friction from either side until it is mature, so a front's resistance is counted once (F_CR is fitted on mature trenches and belts, p. 170); the front lane landed second and added the two conditions in `forces.rs` (2026-10-25, unit test `an_incipient_front_takes_no_forsyth_uyeda_friction`). So off by default (CLAUDE.md: on only with no guard failure on the pool); plates.md "Plate speed, phase 2" has the arm, the probe and the routes that would hold those plates. |
| PLT-600 | collisions | The collision belt's surface from critical taper (`tectonics.collision_belt_taper`, on by default since 2026-10-25). The plate front is the toe of the orogenic wedge. On Earth the plate boundary reaches the surface at the deformation front (the Main Frontal Thrust), and the Lesser Himalaya, though Indian crust, lies above the décollement on the overriding side. The belt's surface rises from the front at the wedge's surface slope alpha (PLT-192) up to the strength height (PLT-253, ISO-021) and is flat behind: S(d) = min(h_s, z_f + d tan alpha), with z_f the mean height of the overriding edge at the front. The step's kept crust raises the ground under S nearest the front first, samples at the same distance alike, until it runs out. So the belt widens as it takes crust in, as a critical wedge grows (width as the square root of the crust taken in), and at the strength height it widens as a plateau. How much stays is unchanged: exactly what the old fill places (PLT-242's share, PLT-243's room share and its repeated passes, PLT-253's cap), to the metre; only where it lands changes. Replaces PLT-243's profile when on. | crates/deeptime-core/src/params.rs: TectonicParams.collision_belt_taper; plates/belts.rs: stack_front, fill_taper; tests the_taper_belt_rises_from_the_front_at_the_slope_and_keeps_the_volume, the_taper_belt_is_lowest_at_the_front_and_keeps_the_steps_crust, the_taper_keeps_what_the_old_fill_keeps_behind_a_sliver, stacking_moves_crust_and_creates_none | S(d) = min(h_s, z_f + 1000 d tan alpha); alpha 1.5 deg (PLT-192); h_s about 5.2 km at 1 g (PLT-253); at z_f 300 m the ramp is 187 km long | m, km | Dahlen 1990, Annu. The growth is self-similar" (p. 60); "it is thus the existence of a brittle-plastic transition at 10-15 km depth that ultimately limits the height of very wide fold-and-thrust belts and accretionary wedges. This provides a natural explanation for the break in topographic slope of the Higher Himalaya at the edge of the Tibetan Plateau" (p. 96). The fill order (nearest first, ties alike), the front as the toe and the strength height as the wedge's height limit are this lane's reading of them, not values from the sources. | PLT-652 | collisions | The overriding plate's share (`tectonics.collision_upper_plate_shortening`, the front lane, 2026-10-25; on by default since the arm fl-upper-unfreeze, 2026-10-25). The belt (the front's orogenic wedge, PLT-600) pushes on the lowland behind it with its gravitational potential energy over the lowland's, F per metre of front (the force a range exerts on its lowlands, Molnar & Lyon-Caen 1988): the balance's edge term (FRC-018) read across the belt, the mean column energy (FRC-014) of the belt's continental samples within `collision_belt_km` over the mean of the strip's (the lowland it pushes on, below), with the edge scale (`forces::belt_push_n_m`; no belt or no strip, no push; the incoming crust as the lowland was tried first and dropped: the wedge fills it to the belt's height and the push vanished in the fixture). Not the balance's buoyancy of the continental lithosphere dragged down (FRC-030): the mantle's hold on the slab, a capacity of the plastic resistance that grows to about 9 × 10¹³ N/m at the slab's full depth (a first build read it as F, and a 150 km plate took all of the convergence from the fixture's fourth step; dropped before any arm). The overriding plate behind the belt carries F as a thin viscous sheet along the strip behind the front: its visible continental samples beyond the belt's zone (the distance field to `collision_belt_km`, PLT-241), between the front's ends along the tangent at its centroid and not ahead of it. Each column shortens at ε̇ = F / (4 η L), PLT-414's law with F for ΔU (L the 100 km lithosphere, FRC-010), with η = η_0 / w and w = e^(−t_eq / τ_s) at its lithosphere's thermal age (PLT-415, ISO-112; PLT-470's form, `thermal::sheet_weakness`, 0 for a sample with no state). The interior shortens at v = F / (4 η_0 L) ∫ w dx (Σ w × a sample's area over the strip's width) and takes the share min(1, v / v_n) of the crust the step keeps. That share goes to the strip's columns in proportion to T w (a thin sheet thickens each column by its own strain, T ε̇), with no strength height (the surface stage spreads it as a plateau's); the wedge and the belt take the rest by their own rules (PLT-242, PLT-244, PLT-600, PLT-601, PLT-253 unchanged). Nothing is created: the interior's crust is the crossing's mix (PLT-248), counted in `kept` and in `plates/collision_upper_km3` (crust budget column `upper`; `upper_km2`, the crossed area times the share, and `upper_most_km2`, the crossed area of fronts whose interior took at least half; written only with the switch). The interior's columns are stamped as a collision orogeny (fold along the front) and kept out of the belt's record. No share is set and the belt sets its own height: a fresh front has no push, the belt rises until its push drives the interior at the rest of the convergence (England & Houseman 1986's plateau); a keeled plate (200 km of lithosphere, a start continent's keel) takes almost none under a belt at its strength height, a hot one (60 km) most or all under a low belt. It is kinematic: the balance and the motion are as before. On the pool (arm fl-upper-unfreeze, 8 runs on the motion fix, against its switch-off twin, which matches m5-unfreeze bit for bit) the interiors took 0.13 of the crust crossed and fronts whose interior took half or more 0.076 of the crossed area; | PLT-655 | subduction | The older side of a subduction initiation's fracture goes under (`tectonics.initiation_polarity_by_age`, the floor-age lane's law 1, 2026-10-25). A side whose fracture samples are half or more continental crust does not go under (as the rule of a new contact reads an overlap); otherwise the side whose fracture floor has the older mean age does, the split on a tie, with both sides continental, or with no fracture found. The planted slab (PLT-270) hangs from that side, the split's or the parent's, and the contact the two plates form later takes the planted slab's polarity (`subduction::planted_polarity`: a new contact between a plate and its parent with a slab one way between them), not the rule of a new contact (the overlap's crust and ages). A correctness fix: off, the slab was always the split's and the contact followed the overlap, so on base15 1,494 of 1,998 such contacts (partings and collisions in the records) put the parent under against the slab, at a median 6 Myr after the split, and the two slabs pulled against each other. | PLT-656 | subduction | A subduction zone propagates along a passive margin (`tectonics.margin_propagation`, the floor-age lane's law 2, 2026-10-25). **Start**: where a plate U's trench (its visible floor with a hidden lattice neighbour of its own group, the TRENCH flag) lies within 1.5 spacings (PLT-534) of U's own coast floor (visible floor with a lattice neighbour of U's continental crust within 1.5 spacings), the floor there is at least 10 Myr old (FRC-005) and U's slab under the overriding plate O is active and at least the critical convergence (PLT-611, 100 km) long, the slab's pull across the margin, FRC-002 × `slab_pull` (PLT-182) × FRC-004's weight at the trench floor's age × min(L, 700 km) / 700 km (PLT-184) × max(cos θ, 0), with θ between the pull (across the strike of U's trench at the trench sample, towards O's visible samples there, PLT-659) and the margin's inward normal at the stretch's end (towards U's continent), is set against the margin's strength there (PLT-658). If it wins, U's continent C and the floor beyond a great-circle tear (a STEP fault) from the stretch's end, away from C, to U's next boundary become a new plate K' with U's motion; U keeps the trench, the slab and the floor between the trench and the tear. **Advance**: the convergence of U on K' across the margin at the tip (K''s coast floor within 1.5 spacings of U's crust, visible or hidden, nearest where K''s rotation carried the last tip) is summed; at each critical convergence (100 km), the pull of U's slab beside the tip, the one under the plate that covers U's crust nearest the tip (K' where that crust is visible, the slab U forms under K'; the nearest other plate where it is hidden: U's slab takes the narrow wedge of floor at the tip down within a step or two where the trench meets the margin at an angle), FRC-002 × FRC-004 at the age of that crust × its length / 700 km × cos θ (θ across the broken stretch towards K''s continent under K', across U's trench towards the other plate), against the strength at the tip moves the next stretch of K''s margin floor to U: the floor from the tip to a tear of its own from the stretch's end (K''s coast floor about one spacing farther along the margin, PLT-659), away from C; the tip moves to that end. A propagation ends when U or K' dies or no tip is found; a passed test the lattice cannot cut cleanly (PLT-659) is a stall and ends it. | PLT-659 | subduction | Law 2's lattice (PLT-656): a start's stretch ends at U's coast floor within 1.6 spacings (PLT-534's 1.5 plus 0.1) of the trench sample, the one farthest from U's trench; a step finds the tip within 2 spacings of where the last left it; the tear is marched in half spacings and cuts the lattice edges that cross its great circle within its length plus half a spacing at each end; each side of a cut keeps at least max(32, `min_plate_area_frac` × n) samples (48 on the quick lattice); a start must leave K' in one piece and U with at most one piece more, and its broken stretch must lie within the stretch end's distance from the trench plus 0.75 spacing; an advance's stretch ends at K''s coast floor within 1.6 spacings of the tip, the one farthest from U, its tear runs from there, it must leave K' and U in no more pieces than before, and its broken stretch must lie within the stretch end's distance from the tip plus 0.75 spacing; the margin's inward normal (the tear's direction, away from it, and the cosines of the force test and the convergence) is across the strike, the principal direction of the plate's coast floor within 2 spacings, signed towards the continent, where that floor reads as a line; a slab's pull at a trench is across the strike of U's trench samples within 4 spacings of the trench sample (twice the margin's window: the sample is at the trench's end and sees its trench on one side only), signed towards the overriding plate's visible samples within 1.5 spacings, which alone lean past the trench's end where U's hidden slab lies over that plate's edge (0.74 against 0.12 for a trench at a right angle to the margin in a test), the same rule for a line (at least three samples, a variance along at least twice the variance across), else the direction to the continent within 1.5 spacings (a point of the margin sees only one or two samples of its continent within them, so that direction alone tilted the test's tear 22° off the margin's normal); hidden samples follow the nearest visible sample of their plate with the same crust. At a resampling, of ocean floor that went under (hidden, or of a plate the top overrides: PLT-440's set), the slab takes the share 0.83 of the sediment and sedimentary pools where the pile there is thicker than 1 km and the top's convergence is slower than 76 mm/yr (an accretionary margin), and all of it otherwise (an erosive margin, and every thinner pile). The same rule holds whatever the top's crust: an intra-oceanic margin's sediment, which PLT-440's 0.12 left 88 % accreted to the oceanic top, follows the criteria on the incoming pile and the convergence, the two Clift & Vannucchi state, and thin piles under oceanic tops (Mariana, Tonga, the Kermadec) are erosive margins in their tables. The pile is the resampled column's own (the blend at its place, sediment plus sedimentary rock); the convergence is the component of the plates' relative motion normal to the front at the top sample (PLT-312's `across`), since Clift & Vannucchi's rate is the orthogonal one: the sample's stamped `convergence_mm_yr` times the cosine of its stamped `obliquity_deg` (`subduction::orthogonal_convergence_mm_yr`; with `DEEPTIME_ARC_NORMAL=1` the stamped rate is that component already). No field of its own: 4 B more per plate sample took full 16k's memory estimate 1.85 MiB past its 5 GiB cap (surface/young.rs `the_estimate_covers_the_measured_hold_closely`). With it on the flat share (PLT-440) is not read.
