| PLT-661 | crust and isostasy (plates) | The margin's sediment is continental (`tectonics.margin_sediment_continental`, the M5 lane, 2026-10-25; off by default: on the arm m5-margins, with PLT-660 and PLT-662, 8 runs against base15, the transition turned 0.32 of the planet continental over the run (supercontinent-1), and the continental share ended at 0.634 against 0.386 (Cogley 1984: 0.41), 0.816 of it under the sea against 0.552 (Cogley: 0.306), the land at 0.114 (target 0.13 to 0.36). The rule ratchets: the resampling's blend mixes the core's crust into a converted ring, which then holds 10 km and more of its own, stops being hyper-extended and is the next ring's margin (plates.md). It stays off until that blend is closed). The rifted-margin taper's ocean side (−W/2 < d < 0 of PLT-261, T* from 20 to 7 km, β from 1.65 to 4.7) is where Earth's rifted margins have their necking and hyper-extended domains, continental crust thinned to under 10 km. A zone member there that is ocean floor and holds rock from the continents (sediment and sedimentary rock beyond the open ocean's pile for the floor's age and latitude: GlobSed's eq. 2a less its pore water, `surface::pelagic::open_floor_solid_m`, ERO-215, since the pelagic lane's merge of 2026-10-25; the surface's pelagic rain lies 14 to 42 m in place under eq. 2a, and what is beyond came down from the land. So that a converted ring is never the next ring's margin (the ratchet), the taper takes a continental column with less than 10 km of its own crystalline crust (`hyper_extended`) as its ocean side: it is no boundary sample of the continental side and the taper's targets and its ocean-crust rule (PLT-294) treat it as ocean floor, and ocean crust the taper adds to it joins its basement. A matured island arc (8 km of its own on its basement) is also the ocean side with the switch on. The zone is found as the step begins, so the step's conversions do not move it. | PLT-663 | ridges and ocean crust | A resampling blends a continental sample's rock pools and its basement only over the corners of its own class: continental and hyper-extended (under 10 km of its own crystalline crust, PLT-661's `hyper_extended`) or continental and not, the class of the old sample nearest the new point (the major corner's until PLT-664, the same day) (the M5 lane, 2026-10-25; on with or without the switches, no switch of its own). Ocean crust's class is its kind, as PLT-293 has it; the sediment pool is still blended over all three corners, and the heat flow over the corners of the sample's kind. A blend over one class is a convex mean of columns on one side of the 10 km line (a column's own crystalline crust is its crystalline pools less its basement, both blended with the same weights), so it stays on that side. Blending over the kind put 10 km or more of a continent's core crust into the converted margin's columns beside it at each resampling (PLT-661: an ocean basement under a thin continental crust): they stopped being hyper-extended, became the taper's continental side, and the next ring, which holds the continents' sediment, turned at the next step, so the margin moved out a ring a resampling (the ratchet of the arm m5-margins). With the switches off the same blend gave an old arc's thin continental crust over ocean crust (0.004 of the planet at the end of the switch-off diagnostic run, supercontinent-1) a share of the thick crust beside it. It changes the ratchet test's zone by under 1 %: after six resamplings, each followed by a step, the hyper-extended count goes 271, 316, 349, 372, 398, 421, 442 (major corner 314, 343, 364, 393, 411, 441). Over 24 resamplings the core's own columns, the continent's crust 35 km thick, wander past the start's edge, 27 after one and 130 after 24, the farthest at z −0.012 then −0.118, the same with the transition's steps or without them: a categorical field resampled from any one old sample moves its edge without bias by a fraction of a spacing each time, so its isolated columns walk, which no choice of corner removes. Four rules were then compared on the quick planet's lattice (100,000 samples, 70 resamplings with no motion, 32 lattice sequences; the coordinator's order: felsic mass kept, then area and edge width, then flecks). Nearest keeps the felsic rock without bias (after 70: supercontinent −0.00 ± 0.27 %, a 2,000 km continent +0.05 ± 5.4, a 500 km micro −4.9 ± 37, strips 1,000 km long and 3, 2 and 1 spacings wide +0.5, +1.6 and +9.0, each within 1.7 standard errors of zero), its edge walking one standard deviation of about 46, 27 and 11 km and leaving 143 flecks and 138 holes on the supercontinent. A vote at half the corners' weight (the median) moves the edge by its curvature: every closed body loses about a quarter of a cell a resampling (the micro 19.4 cells, the continent 18.8), half the micro and 97 % of the one-spacing strips by the 70th, so an island arc's band (about 1.4 spacings) would not last. A conservative remap keeps the felsic rock (−0.7 % on the continent, −3.7 on a micro) on ocean-kind columns, the continental-kind volume −22 and −82 %, against PLT-293. Nearest kept, no rule added; `a_resampling_keeps_thin_crust_on_average` asserts the unbiasedness (24 lattices, the mean within three standard errors). The ratchet's flatness test fails under every rule and stays ignored as the margins stage's acceptance test: that stage, and any one-way conversion measured from the core's edge, waits for an interface-tracking or volume-of-fluid remap with this rule's unbiased mass (the coordinator's call, 2026-10-25)) |

The island arcs' rise follows the same factor; the uplift keeps g (PLT-037). Crust made from ocean floor otherwise (an emerged arc's, a collapse's crystalline rock, a slab's accreted crust) is mostly crystalline rock of its own and stays. The rule holds with the margins switch off as well (the M5 lane, 2026-10-25), so the margin's class is one function of the current state in both directions: PLT-661 turns the zone's ocean side continental (switch on), this turns every other sediment margin back.
